# M3 build log — the full narrative

**Started 2026-10-20,** the day M3's order was decided (D-134), while M2's last
exit criterion, the staging canary's 24 hours (A-35), was still running.
`docs/progress.md` carries the current state and is kept short; the story of M3
lives here. M2's is in [`m2.md`](m2.md), M1's in [`m1.md`](m1.md) and M0's in
[`m0.md`](m0.md). This file follows the same convention as they do: one log per
milestone, under `docs/progress/`, beside the others.

**Why this file is named `m3.md` and not `m0.md`.** `plan-keeper`'s brief,
`/status` and `/bugfix` still say the narrative goes to `docs/progress/m0.md`.
That is the known gap that `m1.md` and `m2.md` also describe (D-088; a fix
needs the owner's approval, because it is under `.claude/`). The task that
wrote this entry named `m3.md` directly, as the convention above asks.

Why each thing was built, and what went wrong on the way. The binding record is
[`plan/decisions.md`](../plan/decisions.md); this is the narrative behind it.
Wrong turns are written down so the next session does not repeat them.

M3 from `plan/10-roadmap.md`: **App MVP, demo-able: GRP-01 to GRP-04, CALL-01 to
CALL-03, JRN-01 to JRN-06, LOST-04, LOST-05, HELP-01; real push to the owner's
phone; SMS to the owner's own number only.** Its exit criteria: **L7 green on
Android (every pull request) and iOS (weekly); the owner can show the app
through internal TestFlight and Google Play internal testing.** The twelve
tasks, in the order the owner accepted, are in D-134 and the roadmap's "M3 — App
MVP in detail".

#### What GRP-01 delivers, in plain words

- A person joins with a **single-use invitation code** that the admin creates
  and sends however they like. It expires 7 days after it was made, by the
  database's clock. The person types their own name when they join (D-136,
  answer 3). The **first admin** joins with a one-time **bootstrap code** that
  Terraform makes and the owner reads once in Clever Cloud's console (answer 1;
  A-36).
- The phone gets a **device credential:** 256 random bits, only their SHA-256
  stored. **Membership is the session** (D-137): a device authenticates while
  its user is a current member, so removing a member signs out every device of
  that user in the same commit, with no revocation flag to forget. A member
  removed **while walking** keeps their own journey to its end, with heartbeats
  and "I'm home" only (`WALKER_ONLY` access), and is signed out when it ends
  (answer 5).
- The admin **lists members and removes one** in a single transaction across
  every journey the member follows, through SM-10's own removal step, with an
  audit row. The walker **removes a responder from their own journey** (D-122's
  route); anyone else gets the one 404.
- A journey's responders must be **current members of the walker's group,**
  held under the lock. The canary's walker may name its own responder and
  nobody else; the canary's users can have no membership (D-128).
- The worker **withholds its check-in** while the SMS check is not reporting
  (AC25), so a worker whose SMS check nobody sees is paged through
  `staging-worker` instead of going unnoticed (D-122's condition for the
  removal route).
- One migration, `0009`: `groups`, `memberships`, `invitations`, `audit_log`.
- In the app: the join screen, the member screen, and for the admin the invite
  and members screens. In Terraform: the bootstrap code. In CI: `android-e2e`
  starts PostgreSQL and the API for the first L7 flows that need a server.
- 33 acceptance criteria (AC1 to AC30 from the spec, AC31 to AC33 added in
  review loop 1). Better Auth is **not** installed: its value is SMS codes and
  passkeys, which come with task 10 and M4 (D-137).

#### Step 1: the safety filter and the owner paths (#94, merged as `60b46ee`)

`modules/groups/` and `adapters/groups.ts` are new owned safety paths. They
decide who may join and who is told when a walker goes silent. As D-128 did for
REL-10, they went into a pull request of their own, merged by hand (D-075),
before GRP-01's own, so that GRP-01's pull request reaches CI's reviewers with
the filter already in place. It came from the second branch
`claude/busy-faraday-40n2zl-ai-review`, as REL-10's did; the owner allowed that
again (D-137).

- `ai-review.yml`'s `safety` filter lists both paths; `scripts/lib/merge-rules.mjs`
  and `.github/CODEOWNERS` need the owner's approval for both. The three lists
  change together because `scripts/gate.test.mjs` holds the filter and
  `OWNER_APPROVAL_PATHS` to each other in both directions (BUG-10, D-097).
- **Red first,** as #94's message records: with only the filter lines,
  "BUG-10: every /apps/ path the safety filter in ai-review.yml lists is a path
  the owner must approve (D-097)" failed; with the owner entries added,
  `scripts/` passed 1,610 of 1,610, `gate:quick` 3 of 3 and `gate:integrity`
  5 of 5.
- **Batched with it (D-075):** Dependabot's #91, four action bumps with the same
  pinned SHAs, each checked against its tag with `git ls-remote`:
  `actions/setup-node` 7.0.0 to 7.1.0; `anthropics/claude-code-action` 1.0.242
  to 1.0.251; `actions/upload-artifact` 7.0.1 to 7.0.2; `actions/setup-java`
  6.0.1 to 6.1.0. The comment beside `--model` now says v1.0.251 installs
  Claude Code 2.1.301. The same number in two test files' header comments is
  test-author's, and followed in GRP-01 (`882ad3a`, comments only).
- The `privacy-security-reviewer`'s notes on #94 were committed as `8c7ffa4`.

This pull request edits `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and **not** `ai-review.yml`
(`git diff --stat origin/main HEAD -- .github`: one file, 63 lines added). By
D-075's reasoning, CI's AI reviewers can therefore run on it. That is not seen
yet.

#### The spec and the decisions

- The spec, [`specs/GRP-01.md`](../specs/GRP-01.md), was drafted with five
  questions for the owner (`a10f246`), then settled on the owner's answers
  (`bce2b88`).
- **D-136 holds the owner's five answers,** asked one at a time in Norwegian,
  each with Claude's recommendation, and the owner chose the recommendation
  each time: (1) the first admin joins with a Terraform-made one-time
  bootstrap code; (2) the new-device notice comes with task 10 and the admin's
  passkey in M4; (3) an invitation is a code only, and the person types their
  own name; (4) a session lasts until the member is removed, with no time limit
  and no rotation in M3; (5) a member removed while walking keeps their own
  journey to its end.
- **D-137 holds the choices delegated to Claude** (D-031), and the seven places
  where the plan disagreed with itself, and how the spec resolves each. It has a
  loop-1 amendment; the loop-2 changes are marked in the spec itself, and D-137
  gained one sentence in `fd8e6e1` (the bootstrap retry's window opens again if
  the admin removes every other member).
- **D-138 makes the app's API client plain `fetch`,** instead of the
  `@orpc/client` and TanStack Query that D-032 names. `code-reviewer` found the
  deviation unrecorded in loop 1. The cost is stated in D-138 and repeated here
  because it is the one a reader should not miss: the success bodies are cast,
  so **the app does not validate the server's answers at run time.** What holds
  the shapes instead is upstream: `api:diff`, the L6 tests of each route's
  answer, and the L7 flows against the real API. It is revisited when an
  answer's shape matters to safety on the phone (an alert, a journey's state).
- D-134 said UI work runs on the Mac (D-055). The owner decided that all of
  GRP-01 is built in the cloud session (D-137, item 5 of the contradictions):
  the app has no iOS configuration until task 12, L5 runs anywhere and L7
  Android runs in CI.

RG-02 order was kept: tests first, then the code.

What loop 1 did, in order (`5db4ecb` the spec, the decisions and the runbooks;
`064be6c` and `b62ce4c` the red tests; then the code):

- **B1 fixed (`3037da9`):** `random_bytes` with `length = 16` and a generation
  keeper, as the canary's credential has (D-128's loop-1 amendment). Its `hex`
  is sensitive and it has no ID made from the value; the app's environment
  takes `upper(random_bytes.admin_bootstrap_code.hex)`, the same 32 characters.
  Rotating the code is raising `local.admin_bootstrap_code_generation` in a
  pull request, because `infra-staging.yml` has no `-replace`. `terraform fmt
  -check` and `validate` passed (1.16.4). The reviewer re-ran its scratch plan
  on `3fac8fb`: `random_bytes` prints no code, and a rotation plan shows only
  the keeper. For any secret Terraform makes from now on: `random_password` or
  `random_bytes`, never `random_id`, `random_string` or `random_integer`.
- **RG-05 fixed (`d4eaabb`, `6b03fb3`):** `modules/groups/` joined
  `SAFETY_PATHS` as a folder, with a mutation group of its own after
  `journeys`, and `groups.system.test.ts` joined the `journeys` group's tests,
  fourth. `adapters/groups.ts` stays out (L3 and the shared suite prove it,
  D-095's reading).
  - **A wrong turn, kept so it is not repeated:** the group was first named
    `groups`, as the spec said, and that cannot pass BUG-45-AC8 in
    `scripts/gate.test.mjs`, which refuses any mutation group's name as a whole
    word in `ci.yml`'s three mutation jobs, where `groups` is the plan's output
    key (`needs.mutation-plan.outputs.groups`). The implementer found the
    conflict between tests and stopped. The coordinator chose to rename the
    group **`membership`** and keep BUG-45-AC8 unchanged (`6f31bfe`, `639fc12`,
    `6b03fb3`), not to narrow the test's matcher (RG-03).
- **Should-fixes done:**
  - the bootstrap join can be retried with its join key while the admin is the
    group's only member (`bad3a32`): the group's row keeps the SHA-256 of the
    first admin's join key, in migration `0009` itself (no `0010`, because
    `0009` was on no deployed database); a lost answer (F2) had left the group
    made and the owner outside it;
  - the bootstrap code is compared as SHA-256 digests by
    `crypto.timingSafeEqual`, never `===`;
  - the display-name rule, once, in `packages/contracts/src/display-name.ts`
    (AC31): it refuses bidirectional controls, other format characters except
    ZWNJ and ZWJ (real Persian names and joined emoji need them), and names with
    nothing visible; the app runs the same function before it sends;
  - `android-e2e` runs on changes under `apps/server/` and `packages/contracts/`
    (AC33, `d4eaabb`);
  - the `responderIds` description says "a member of the walker's group";
  - the app's accessibility items (`5097357`): visible labels, a waiting
    indicator, the removal confirmation in the member's own row, the code shown
    in whole groups and read character by character, one "Prøv igjen";
  - the test-auditor's four tests (`b62ce4c`, `064be6c`): the app reuses its join
    key, gives up at exactly 20 s, the normaliser's ASCII filter, the closure
    route in AC21's list. Several passed on arrival, and were shown to fail on
    planted faults in a scratch copy.
- **`3fac8fb`, a gate caught a gap:** `gate:full` failed the coverage ratchet.
  `modules/groups/` had become a safety path, and `join.ts` stood at 9 of 10

- **Then all five reviewers passed `3fac8fb`** (the spec's record at `fd8e6e1`;

#### Review loop 2 (`fd8e6e1` to `25ca436`, from the delta reviews of `3fac8fb`)

Nothing blocked. Claude's choices (D-031): the cheap should-fixes go in now,
and the rest are recorded as left for later tasks. The spec's table "Review
loop 2" has each with its reviewer.

Done:

- **The code's spoken name** (`a11y-i18n-reviewer` S1; AC28): the invitation
  code's screen-reader label, and what is announced when it appears, begin with
  the code field's own name and a colon ("Invitasjonskode: K 7 Q 2 M, ..."). A
  spelled-out label needs a name in front. The L7 flow's patterns begin with
  `${CODE_LABEL}:` and still write no text a reader sees.
- **The removal's indicator in the confirmation** (S2; AC30): feedback belongs
  where the admin acted, so while a removal is in flight the waiting indicator
  shows inside the confirmation, after its two buttons, which stay on screen,
  disabled. The list's own indicator is for loading the list only.
- **`accessible` on the indicator** (S3; AC30): the indicator itself is an
  accessibility element with its role and label, so a screen reader stops on
  it.
- **Any-4xx refusal** (`code-reviewer`; AC28): a removal refused with any 4xx
  but 401, a 404 without `MEMBER_NOT_FOUND` among them, says it was refused and
  the member stays listed. The screen already did this, so the new tests were
  green on arrival; the members screen's header comment, which had called such
  a 404 "no answer from the route", now says what the code does.
- **The infra test also refuses `random_string` and `random_integer`**
  (`privacy-security-reviewer`, note 3; AC8): their values are printed by a plan
  too.
- **`invitation_failed` pinned at L2** (`test-auditor` S2; AC24): an invitation
  the store cannot create writes one `invitation_failed` line with
  `stage: 'store'`.
- **D-137:** the bootstrap retry's window opens again if the admin removes every
  other member (`privacy-security-reviewer`, note 1).
- **The confirmation holds while in flight** (`25ca436`, after the
  `a11y-i18n-reviewer`'s pass of `879096f`): nothing pinned what the members
  screen does while a removal is in flight. One L5 test holds the DELETE and
  checks that confirm and cancel both stay on screen, disabled; that pressing
  confirm again sends no second DELETE; that pressing cancel takes away neither
  the confirmation nor its indicator; and that, once the answer is released,
  the member leaves the list, the removal is announced naming them, and exactly
  one DELETE was sent. The behaviour already existed, so it passes on `HEAD`
  (I ran it: 101 of 101 in the feature's Jest suite). The test-author planted
  faults in a scratch copy and reported that it fails on a cancel left enabled
  while busy, on a confirm left enabled while busy, and on a `Pressable` left
  pressable under a disabled state. **This commit is test-only and was reviewed
  by none of the five.**

**Tried and withdrawn** (`1594939`, then `b260fca`, `7f81b39`, `879096f`): the
`test-auditor`'s S1 asked that `join.ts`'s unreachable `code === null` guard be
killed inside its own mutation run. The way to do that is to run `join.test.ts`
(L2) in the `membership` group before `groups.system.test.ts`, and that needs a
configuration that holds unit and system files, `vitest.coverage.config.mjs`.
The implementer built it (`1594939`) and measured it, fresh and alone: 100
mutants, 92 killed, in 48 s, `invitations.ts` 93.10 %, `join.ts` 97.22 %,
`members.ts` 85.71 %. It was **not green,** and was not worked around: **a
group's configuration is a mutation input** (`decideMutation`), so
`vitest.coverage.config.mjs` became one, and two BUG-14 tests ask for it by
name, in `ai-review.yml`'s safety filter (merged by hand and batched, D-075;
for GRP-01 changed from its own branch and pull request) and in
`.claude/agents/safety-reviewer.md`'s own list of inputs (the owner's to
approve, and nothing under `.claude/` is the implementer's to write). Claude
weighed that cost against what it buys, one guard no request can reach whose
removal fails loudly (a `join_failed` line and a 500, never a join), with
`join.ts` already at 83.3 %, above D-098's 80 %, and withdrew the change:
the spec (`b260fca`), the tests' pins (`7f81b39`, identical to `639fc12` again)
and the code (`879096f`, a revert). The change goes with the `api.ts` bug,
which needs a group over the system tests and the same two files anyway: **BUG-57.**
`test-auditor` agreed (`15e66fe`): withdrawing S1 is acceptable under RG-05 and
D-098, since every membership file is at 80 % or above and both behaviours are
pinned at L2; the deferral needs an ID when it is queued, which BUG-57 is.

#### Gates and measurements: what was verified where

#### The synthetic key that gitleaks found, and the exception (`a9b9dd1`, `8ac9697`, `0019d17`, `28aa12d`)

CI's required `security` job failed: gitleaks 8.30.1 found a `generic-api-key`
at `packages/contracts/src/groups.test.ts:34` in `064be6c`, the commit that
wrote a synthetic join key there as a literal. It was never a credential of
anything, but RG-07 (test data is always synthetic) and D-089 ask for
credentials made at run time, because gitleaks and the write-time scan read
every committed line.

- **`a9b9dd1`, the fix in the tree:** the join key is `randomBytes(32)` as
  base64url (43 characters, which the contract's pattern takes); the first
  admin's bootstrap code in `domain/groups.test.ts` is the test kit's
  `syntheticBootstrapCode()`; the three spoilt codes in `config.test.ts` are made
  from one such code, each keeping its one fault (computed above the
  `test.each` table, because the RG-03 counter does not count a `test.each`
  whose table holds a `)`); the code handed to Maestro in `e2e-android.test.mjs`
  is `randomBytes(16)` as upper-case hex; the mobile test's invitation code is
  drawn from Crockford's characters. Alphabet runs, the mobile test's
  device credential and SHA-256 known-answer vectors were left as written, on
  purpose (the commit says why). No assertion changed.
- **The commit that added the literal stays in the branch's history,** and that
  history is what the security job scans. **`8ac9697`** adds `.gitleaksignore`
  with **one full fingerprint** (`commit:file:rule:line`), so the same value
  written again anywhere is still found. `privacy-security-reviewer` checked it
  with scratch commits: the same value at the same line in a new commit, at
  another line, and in another file were all found. gitleaks 8.30.1 reports no
  leaks over history and tree with it.
- `0019d17` commits that reviewer's notes: the single fingerprint is the
  narrowest exception, and `.gitleaksignore` and `.gitleaks.toml` should be
  code-owner paths. That became SEC-06 (next section).
- **Follow-up, tracked (asked for by `code-reviewer`):** `064be6c` is a
  branch-only commit and main takes pull requests squashed, so once #96 merges
  the line matches nothing on `main`. **Remove that line, and the comment above
  it, in a pull request after the merge.** `/.gitleaksignore` is a code-owner
  path now, so the owner approves it. It has a row in `docs/progress.md`.
- **A cost this caused (`d2c22ac`, `28aa12d`):** `a9b9dd1`'s five comments cited
  SM-01's "Synthetic data only", and `req:coverage` counts any test file that
  names an ID, so SM-01 ("One active journey per walker") went from 13 to 17
  test files with no SM-01 assertion behind them. `test-auditor` found it. The
  rule meant is RG-07, which `req:coverage` does not collect. All five comments
  and `.gitleaksignore`'s now cite RG-07, and SM-01 is back at 13. (Five files,
  four counted: `e2e-android.test.mjs` starts with `// req-coverage:
  fixtures-only`.)
- **`a4faed8` and `a8876e1`, two small ones.** CI's traceability check failed on
  the 112 note because a changed spec named CALL-01 and CALL-02, which GRP-01
  does not implement and no test names; the note now points to the call stories
  in `01b-mvp-scope.md` without naming the IDs (the gotcha in `docs/progress.md`
  about naming a requirement with no test yet). The note itself came from
  `a11y-i18n-reviewer`: a signed-in phone opens on GRP-01's member screen,
  which has no 112 button until task 2's home screen replaces it.

#### SEC-06: `/.gitleaksignore` and `/.gitleaks.toml` are code-owner paths (`5d0bf20` red, `155fef6` green)

`privacy-security-reviewer` raised this twice on #96, the pull request that adds
`.gitleaksignore`. CI's `security` check runs `./gitleaks git .` with no
`--config` (`ci.yml`, "Secret scan (gitleaks)"), so gitleaks takes both files
from the pull request's own tree. With no code owner, a pull request could add a
fingerprint or an allowlist that silences the scan on itself, and pass.

- **Red, `5d0bf20`:** two tests in `scripts/lib/merge-rules.test.mjs`, beside
  REL-10-AC18's pins: `OWNER_APPROVAL_PATHS` holds both paths, and
  `.github/CODEOWNERS` gives each a line naming `@bvst @urso-agent`, with
  `reviewCodeowners` finding every owner path owned. Both failed on their
  assertions; the other 49 in the file passed. Named SEC-06 because its
  mitigation is "automated dependency and secret scanning on every change", and
  an unowned ignore list is how that is switched off.
- **Green, `155fef6`:** both paths in `OWNER_APPROVAL_PATHS` and in CODEOWNERS.
  `/.gitleaks.toml` does not exist yet; it is owned before it exists, since the
  pull request that adds one is the one to stop. The implementer made the
  change; a container restart stopped it before the commit, so the main session
  checked and committed it: `merge-rules`, `gate` and `gate-integrity` tests 283
  of 283, `gate:file` on `merge-rules.mjs` passing, and CI-01 5 of 5 against the
  live repository (all the commit's report).
- I read `.github/CODEOWNERS`: `/.gitleaksignore` and `/.gitleaks.toml` each
  have a line with `@bvst @urso-agent`.

#### BUG-61: vite 8.3.4 for GHSA-587h-7wmj-h5pm (`6b7cd97`, `dbe2c7a`)

- **What turned red:** CI's required `security` check failed on `155fef6` at
  `pnpm audit --audit-level high`: "16 vulnerabilities found", "12 moderate | 4
  high (2 ignored)", where it had read "2 high (2 ignored)" earlier that day. The
  new advisory is GHSA-587h-7wmj-h5pm in `vite` >=8.3.0 <=8.3.2, patched in
  8.3.3, reached through `.>vitest>vite` and `apps__server>vitest>vite`. It was
  published after the branch's last green audit, so `main` is exposed the same
  way; that is `6b7cd97`'s reasoning, and `main`'s own audit run was not read.
  The pull request carries the fix, as BUG-23 carried `source-map-js`.
- **The fix, `6b7cd97`:** a root dev dependency, `vite: ^8.3.3`. `vite` comes in
  only as an auto-installed peer of `vitest` and `@vitest/mocker`, and it is the
  only way found to move that resolution. A pnpm override changed only the peer
  range ("unmet peer vite@^8.3.3: found 8.3.0"), and `pnpm up --depth Infinity`
  left vite at 8.3.0. Both instances now resolve to vite 8.3.4 (2026-10-08),
  with `rolldown` 1.2.9 to 1.2.14, `nanoid` 3.3.20 and `postcss` 8.5.31. No
  other package changed. Reported in the commit: the audit shows only the 2
  ignored advisories; `licenses:check` passes with 1031 dependencies; Vitest on
  vite 8.3.4 passes 2613 tests in 57 server, packages and gate files, and 842 of
  842 system tests.
- **Pinned, `dbe2c7a`** (`test-auditor`'s should-fix: `PATCHED` had rows for
  BUG-23 and BUG-24 and none for this):
  - a `PATCHED` row in `scripts/dependency-audit.test.mjs`, `vulnerableFrom:
    null` and `fixedAt: '8.3.3'`: every vite below 8.3.3 fails. It is `null`,
    not 8.3.0, because npm's bulk advisory data (queried 2026-10-20) gives this
    advisory one range per release line (>=7.0.0 <=7.3.6, >=8.0.0 <=8.1.5,
    >=8.2.0 <=8.2.3, >=8.3.0 <=8.3.2) and other high advisories cover 8.0.x, so
    a floor at 8.3.0 would pass a vite 8.2.3 that the audit fails. The check can
    be red where the audit is not (the older lines' own fixed releases), never
    green where it is red;
  - a test that holds the root `package.json`'s `devDependencies.vite` at a
    floor of 8.3.3 or later. Its failure message says why the line is there and
    what to show before dropping it: that the lockfile resolves no vite below
    8.3.3 without it.
  - Planted in scratch copies (the commit's report): HEAD's files, 24 of 24;
    HEAD's `package.json` with `155fef6`'s lockfile (vite 8.3.0), the row fails;
    both of `155fef6`'s files, the row and the dev dependency pin fail; HEAD's
    lockfile with the vite line dropped from `package.json`, only the dev
    dependency pin fails, and the lockfile check alone would have stayed green.
  - RG-03: two existing tests extended, none weakened; the file goes from 21
    tests to 24. The per-control field `override` is renamed `fixedBy`, because
    this fix wrote a dev dependency, not an override.
- **Status:** fixed in #96, to merge with it. It has a row in `docs/progress.md`.

#### INF-06: a failed flow's reason reaches the job log (`8956193` red, `faa0ed7` green)

#96's `android-e2e` failed in `first-admin-invites` and the job log said only "1
of 3 flows failed or did not finish: first-admin-invites (ERROR)". Maestro writes
the reason in `report.xml`, inside the debug artifact, and **this session cannot
download CI artifacts** (the commit says a cloud session cannot; the brief for
this record says the network policy refuses GitHub's blob host, which I did not
test). Without a change, the cause was unreadable.

- **Red, `8956193`:** 13 new tests in `scripts/lib/e2e-android.test.mjs`, 11 of
  them failing at their first assertion on the reason (INF-06-AC9 asks for "a
  message saying which one"); the other two are guards, green on purpose (a
  cancelled flow with no `<failure>`, and a passing run, give today's message). The fixture's shape was read from Maestro 2.10.0's
  `JUnitTestSuiteReporter.kt` at tag `cli-2.10.0`: a failed flow's reason is the
  text of its `<failure>` element, after any `<properties>`. 341 lines added,
  none removed.
- **Green, `faa0ed7`:** `judgeReport` (`scripts/lib/e2e-android.mjs`) also reads
  each flow's `<failure>` and prints its reason under the flow's name, below the
  summary line. A reason is the `message` attribute followed by the text, both
  when both exist; the five named XML entities and numeric ones are decoded once
  (`&amp;lt;` becomes `&lt;`); each flow's reason is cut on its own at **500
  characters** with a note that names `reports/e2e-android/report.xml`; a
  self-closing `<testcase>` (a cancelled flow) never takes the next flow's
  `<failure>`. A flow with no reason, and a passing run, give exactly the old
  message. Typed values are not redacted: in CI the only typed value is the
  job's synthetic bootstrap code, which the log already prints. 162 of 162.

#### The `android-e2e` failure and its fix (`b571158`)

**Cause.** With the reason in the log, the failure read (job 118918227742):

```
maestro.js.JsEvaluationException: SyntaxError: <eval>:1:19 Missing space
after numeric literal
CODE_LABEL}:\s*[0-9A-HJKMNP-TV-Z](\s+[0-9A-HJKMNP-TV-Z]){4}(\s*[,.;]\s*...){3
```

Maestro evaluates `${…}` in a flow string as JavaScript, **greedily.** Its
`Env.kt` (`maestro-orchestra-models`, tag `cli-2.10.0`, and the release jar this
repository pins, checked against `MAESTRO_SHA256`) holds the regex
`(?<!\\)\$\{([^$]*)}`: the script runs from `${` to the **last** `}` before the
next `$`, not to the first `}`. Loop 2's `${CODE_LABEL}:` prefix stood before a
regex quantifier `{4}` and `{3}` in two strings (the label's wait and the
group's `childOf`), and no `$` came after, so Maestro took `CODE_LABEL}:\s*…{4}){3`
as one script and failed on it. **Every `android-e2e` run of #96 failed at that
step.**

**A wrong turn, kept so it is not repeated.** GRP-01-AC28's `patterns()` in
`scripts/lib/e2e-android.test.mjs` had read `${NAME}` up to its own `}`, which is
not what Maestro does, so `faa0ed7`'s test file (162 of 162) passed on the broken
flows as well as the fixed ones, and that is how the broken flows got through. A
test that models a tool wrongly passes on code the tool rejects. The fix models
Maestro's own reading.

**Fix.**
- `apps/mobile/e2e/first-admin-invites.yaml`: both label patterns are written
  out in full, with no `{4}` or `{3}` (five characters with `\s+` between them,
  and three more groups after `\s*[,.;]\s*`). One pattern per step, as before.
  The group's own `{5}` stays, because that string holds no `${`. A comment
  quotes Maestro's regex and says why.
- The harness's `asMaestroInterpolates` reads `${…}` with Maestro's two patterns
  (the second turns `\${…}` back into `${…}`): a bare name becomes its `-e`
  value, and any other script, or a name no `-e` passes, throws and names what
  Maestro would evaluate. GRP-01-AC28's `patterns()` uses it.
- **New guard, INF-06-AC11:** no string in a flow holds a `${…}` beside any
  other brace, so Maestro reads each as a bare name. It reads every flow under
  `apps/mobile/e2e/`, and fails if it reads no `${` at all.

**Evidence** (the commit's report; I re-ran none of it):
- Red on `faa0ed7`'s flows in a scratch copy: 20 failed, 143 passed, the guard
  naming `first-admin-invites.yaml:81` and `:91`. With the new flows 163 of 163.
- A scratch Java program called Maestro 2.10.0's own `evaluateScripts` over the
  58 flow lines holding `${`: the old `:81` and `:91` threw the job log's error
  word for word; the other 56, the two new labels included, evaluate.
- Same meaning: the old pattern with `{4}` and `{3}` expanded by text
  substitution is character for character the new one; 200,000 random labelled
  texts matched the same under both (14,829 matched by both); the 19
  GRP-01-AC28 tests pass.
- `e2e-android.test.mjs` 163 of 163; with `scripts/e2e-android.test.mjs` 194 of
  194; the other four test files that name the flows' path 120 of 120;
  `tests:changes`: none weakened.
- RG-03: the patterns change spelling, not meaning; no test or assertion removed
  or loosened; the file goes from 162 tests to 163.

**L7 itself runs only in CI's `android-e2e`.** Whether it now passes is **not
known here.** Until that run is read, "fixed" means the harness and the flows
agree with Maestro's own code, not that the flow passes on an emulator. The
earlier job log reported "1 of 3 flows failed or did not finish", so the other
two flows were not the failure; the steps of `first-admin-invites` after the
failing one have not run in CI, as far as the commits show.

#### Test-only additions

- **`ae55f93`, the contract pinned to the domain** (`code-reviewer`'s
  should-fix): the roles are written in `packages/contracts/src/groups.ts` and
  again in `apps/server/src/domain/groups.ts` (the database's `membership_role`
  enum is made from the domain's), and the invitation code's shape is written
  as the contract's `INVITATION_CODE_PATTERN` and as the domain's `TYPED_CODE`
  and `WELL_FORMED_CODE`. Nothing failed if one copy changed. New file
  `apps/server/src/groups-contract.test.ts` (L2, 31 tests; in the server, outside
  `domain/`, because the contracts package may import neither the server nor the
  test kit, AR-07): the contract's `ROLES` equals the domain's in order, and
  what the join's answer and the member list carry takes exactly those roles;
  for any string the contract's pattern takes it exactly when the domain's
  normaliser reads it as a code (fast-check, 2000 runs, and 27 named cases). **The
  one difference is pinned as it is:** the field's `.max(100)`; a code padded
  past 100 characters is one the domain would read and the contract refuses as
  the fixed 400. The spec names no cap. Six planted faults in scratch copies
  were each caught. GRP-01 goes from 51 to 52 test files.
- **`dd7126d`, the race that the `for update` mutant survived**
  (`safety-reviewer`'s should-fix): the planted fault, `for update` taken out of
  `removeMember`'s read of the member (`adapters/groups.ts`, about line 369),
  survived every L3 test. Without it, a second removal of the same member,
  arriving while the first is in flight, reads the row the first has deleted but
  not committed, then throws "The membership was not deleted ..." and the API
  answers 500 `member_removal_failed` instead of 404 `MEMBER_NOT_FOUND`. New in
  `apps/server/src/groups.integration.test.ts`, GRP-01-AC16 and AC17: a second
  connection holds the journey's row, so the first removal stops holding M's
  membership row; the second removal is started; both waits are read in
  `pg_stat_activity`, not guessed by a sleep; then the holder lets go. The
  answers must be `[removed, not_removed MEMBER_NOT_FOUND]`, neither throws, and
  one `MEMBER_REMOVED` audit row is written. It lives in the L3 file, not the
  shared group-store suite, because the in-memory fake cannot wait on a row. On a
  throwaway PostgreSQL 16.13 (CI runs 15; CI's `integration` job is the real
  run): green 5 runs of 5, and red 5 runs of 5 with the `for update` removed, at
  the answers. No existing test changed.
- **`d2c22ac`:** comments only, five test files, RG-07 for SM-01 (above).

- **Every count and every red or green in this entry** is the committing

- **`main` is exposed to BUG-61** by the commit's reasoning only; `main`'s own

#### Left for later, from #96's CI reviewers

I checked the spec's "Left for later tasks" first. It already has the dark-first
screens, the 401 and the walking removed member, the combining-mark names, the
`api.ts` mutation gap and the iOS items, and **none of the items below is in
it.** Two touch other parts of the spec, and say so in their items: the `as T`
casts (approach 14, accepted in general) and the missing index (a loop-1 note,
optional). None has a bug ID; whether any becomes one is the owner's call (no ID,
no work).

For the next tasks:
- **Task 3 (start a journey): the join response is not checked in the app.**
  `callApi` casts a success body `as T` (`apps/mobile/src/shared/api/client.ts`,
  line 104, read). D-138 accepts that, and says it is revisited when an answer's
  shape matters to safety on the phone; `code-reviewer` assigns the join's
  answer to task 3. (The spec's approach 14 records the accepted casts in
  general, not this item.)
- **Task 2 (the home screen) or the next feature that needs them: `ui.tsx`'s
  primitives should move to `shared/ui`.** They are in `features/grp/ui.tsx`
  today. Move them when a second feature needs them; task 2 is also the one that
  makes the kit dark-first (the spec has that part).
- **The invitation code is grouped in two places:** the server formats it
  (`modules/groups/invitations.ts` line 34, "in groups of five, hyphens
  between") and the app groups what it is given (`invite-screen.tsx` line 33).
  Whether a test holds the two together I did not look for.
- **`stage` has one value.** `join_failed`, `invitation_failed` and
  `member_removal_failed` each take `stage: 'store'` only (`ports.ts` lines
  823-825, read), so the field carries nothing yet. The other failure events have
  two or three. Give it a second value when a second place can fail, or drop it.
- **`removeInside`'s boundary is a comment.** It is exported from
  `adapters/journeys.ts` for the admin's removal, and its doc comment says "Only
  `adapters/groups.ts` calls it from outside this file" (lines 937-938, read).
  The reviewer's finding is that nothing but the comment holds that.

From `safety-reviewer`:
- **The credential check's journey join has no index on `journeys.device_id`.**
  `adapters/device-credentials.ts` line 78 left-joins `journeys` on
  `device_id` and the unended states, and every authenticated request runs it. The
  only index on unended journeys is the walker's (`journeys_one_unended_per_walker`,
  `db/schema.ts` line 150). The spec's loop-1 note allows matching on `walker_id`
  too, so that index serves it, "only if it stays one query and simple" (lines
  378-383); the adapter matches on `device_id` alone. Cost not measured.
- **The admin's removal holds journey rows while it waits.** As the reviewer put
  it; I did not trace the lock order. `removeInside` sets a lock timeout at its
  start (`adapters/journeys.ts` line 944), which is where to look first.

From `privacy-security-reviewer`:
- **A CI check that refuses an inline `gitleaks:allow`.** As I read the reason: an
  inline comment silences a finding on its own line, in a file that is not a
  code-owner path, and so gets round SEC-06's owner gate on `.gitleaksignore`.
  `ci.yml` is under `/.github/`, so the owner approves the change.
- **`pnpm-lock.yaml` and `pnpm-workspace.yaml` have no code owner.** I read
  `.github/CODEOWNERS`: neither has a line, and there is no catch-all.
  `pnpm-workspace.yaml` is BUG-25 already (queued); the lockfile is added to
  BUG-25's row in `docs/progress.md`. BUG-61's fix edits the lockfile.

From `test-auditor`:
- **RG-06: random test values are unseeded.** `a9b9dd1` made more of them
  (`randomBytes`, the test kit's `syntheticBootstrapCode()`, `Math.random` in the
  mobile test), and a failure on one draw cannot be re-run on the same draw. A
  seed printed with a failure would let it be replayed (Claude's suggestion, not
  the reviewer's).

#### BUG-62, new: `hono`'s advisory GHSA-5r4p-p66f-jhc7

- `pnpm audit` reports a moderate advisory, GHSA-5r4p-p66f-jhc7, in `hono`
  >=4.12.4 <4.13.11, patched in 4.13.11. `apps/server` depends on `hono` `^4.13.8`,
  installed as 4.13.8, a production dependency of the API.
- The advisory (CWE-174, created 2026-10-20) is about `serveStatic` decoding the
  request path a second time, so middleware mounted on a static prefix can be
  bypassed. "Applications that serve only public files are not affected."
- `apps/` does not use `serveStatic` (a grep finds no use), so it is not
  exploitable in the API today. CI's audit runs at `--audit-level high`, so it is
  not red.
- Queued for the owner to schedule. Claude recommends bumping `hono` to
  `^4.13.11` in a small `/bugfix`, or with the next dependency batch, so a later
  use of `serveStatic` does not inherit it.

- **The correction of item 11.** In its delta review of `93047f8`,
  `privacy-security-reviewer` raised one should-fix on D-139's item 11, the reason
  for `redact.ts`. It cited "(D-068, PRIV-07)", but PRIV-07 covers only precise

  and D-068 says the password is a SEC-03 matter. It also said `redact.ts` removes

  file's own header contradicts: it lists what it does not cover (D-077), namely

  and says "the failure lines `process.ts` and `worker.ts` write through
  `describeFailure`", with a visible "Corrected 2026-10-21" note in its amendment.
  The owner's answer, the code owner and the filter are unchanged.
  `docs/progress.md` had a shorter form of the same wording ("strips the database

### 2026-10-22 — BUG-17: e2e:android switches the emulator to bokmål itself

**Where it stands, written so that it is true now.** *(Later the same day this
changed: [#99](https://github.com/bvst/TryggHverdag/pull/99) was merged at
10:20:14 UTC on 2026-10-22 as `3d2255c`, inside window B, and its deploy cost no
canary run. The entry of 2026-10-23, at the end of this file, is the current
state, with the merge's results and the pull request's `android-e2e` evidence;
the bullets below, "Nothing may merge" included, are as they stood when this
was written.)*

- BUG-17 is **fixed on the session branch `claude/busy-faraday-40n2zl` (base `main`
  at `a2457b5`; the fix is `9caa720`) and is not merged.** When this entry was written
  no pull request was open for it (the GitHub API listed one open pull request,
  Dependabot's #95, and none from this branch). The decision is **D-140**, Claude's
  choice under D-031.
- **Nothing may merge to `main` before 12:22:54 UTC on 2026-10-22,** this fix
  included: window B of REL-10-AC19 ends then, and a deploy restarts the worker.
  That time, and the rule, are the coordinating session's; before this record, no
  file in `docs/` held them (a search for "window B" and "12:22" found nothing).
- The pull request's own `android-e2e` run is the first proof that the fix works
  on CI's image. By `scripts/lib/affected.mjs` (I read the code; I ran no pull
  request), a change to `.github/workflows/ci.yml` makes `touchesApp` true, so that
  job's flows step runs on the pull request, before any merge.

**How this record was made.** The evidence, the test counts and the gate results
were checked by the coordinating session on 2026-10-22 and are repeated here as
they were reported. I ran only `pnpm run req:coverage` and `pnpm run
tests:changes`, read the diffs of the branch, and asked GitHub's API about pull
requests #95, #97 and #98 and whether one is open for the branch. What I checked
myself is in "What is unverified here".

#### The evidence

- **Three failures before the install:** #57 (job 110875720264), #60 (job
  111180346250) and #96 (job 118947039610, run 39515647691). Each said "Waited 120 s
  for the emulator to be in Norwegian bokmål with its package service up … its
  package service was found, and its configuration's language was en-rUS".
- **The failing run and its passing re-run, compared.** The runner's log of job
  118947039610 (failed) and of job 119070015215 (the owner's re-run, which passed)
  has the same sequence at the same timing: "Boot completed", then "Changing
  locale to nb-NO … Restarting framework", then "Disabling animations" with three
  `adb shell settings put`, then `e2e:android`. So that sequence does not tell the
  two apart.
- **In the failing run,** the framework was up, and still `en-rUS`, 130 s after
  boot.
- **What the old record said and this does not settle.** #96 did not change the
  locale wait (`checkLocale` and `waitForLocale` were not in its diff, and its
  `ci.yml` diff changed no emulator setting). Since #96 the API and PostgreSQL also
  run on that job's runner, which adds load while the emulator restarts its
  framework; whether that makes the failure likelier is not known, since it
  happened twice before they existed. The fix does not depend on the answer, and
  does not give it.

#### The root cause, as far as the evidence goes

- CI started the emulator with `-change-locale nb-NO`. The emulator applies that
  asynchronously after boot, by restarting Android's framework, and `e2e:android`
  could only wait for the result: the switch was not the script's, and it cannot
  be watched.
- In the failing run, **either the switch did not take, or the restart did not
  happen. Which of the two cannot be known:** the script logged nothing of its
  readings (its timeout message gave only the last one), and the emulator's switch
  is not observable. The runner's own log is the same in a run that failed and a
  run that passed.
- So the cause is a switch made outside the script, asynchronously and out of
  sight, that sometimes did not take. **Why it sometimes does not take is not
  known.**

#### The fix (D-140)

- **The switch is the script's.** Inside its existing wait, if the first reading
  is not ready (the configuration is not bokmål, or the package service is not
  found), `e2e:android` runs, once each, `adb root`, `adb wait-for-device`,
  `adb shell setprop persist.sys.locale nb-NO` and `adb shell setprop ctl.restart
  zygote`. Then it waits as before: 120 s from the first reading, until the
  configuration is bokmål and the package service is found. In the code:
  `switchToBokmal` in `scripts/lib/e2e-android.mjs`, called through a new
  `switchLocale` option of `waitForLocale`, which `scripts/e2e-android.mjs` passes.
- **A device already in bokmål, with its package service up, is not switched:** it
  passes on its first reading. **One edge, stated plainly:** the code's test is "not
  ready", so a device in bokmål whose package service is not found at the first
  reading (its framework mid-restart) is switched too, and a first reading that
  adb could not make fails at once at `adb root`, naming it. The tests pin the
  device that is ready; I found none for the edge. The coordinating session's brief
  said "not bokmål"; D-140 says "not ready", which is what the code does.
- **A refused step fails at once,** naming the step and what adb printed: a status
  that is not 0 (null included), or `adb root` saying "cannot run as root", which
  adb answers with status 0.
- **The timeout message** now also says what `persist.sys.locale` said last, and
  whether the framework was seen restarting during the wait (the package service
  "not found" in any reading). The script reads the property at every reading, for
  that message and for nothing else.
- **CI.** `ci.yml` drops `-change-locale` from both emulator steps, and drops
  `EMULATOR_LOCALE` from the job's environment and from the AVD cache key. So every
  run takes the script's switch, not only a run where the emulator's switch fails,
  and a fresh snapshot is made without it. The flows step is renamed "Boot the
  emulator, switch it to bokmål and run every flow".
- **Why not a fallback.** A fallback taken only when the emulator's switch fails
  would be an untested path. D-081 (run 8) had already found that `-prop
  persist.sys.locale` is refused by the emulator ("only 'qemu.*' properties are
  supported"). On the first boot in bokmål, installs are refused for a while,
  which the install wait already handles.
- **The cost, stated plainly.** The owner's Mac runs (INF-06-AC16) need an image
  that allows `adb root`. A `google_apis` image does; a Play Store image does not,
  and the script then fails at once, naming `adb root`.

#### Test changes (RG-03)

The new tests are `scripts/lib/e2e-android.test.mjs` (the steps, the refusals and
the timeout message), a `describe` in `scripts/e2e-android.test.mjs` (the script
run against a stand-in `adb`, with a new device that takes the switch only from
the script), and one test in `scripts/gate.test.mjs` (no emulator step in `ci.yml`
is started with `-change-locale`).

Existing code in `scripts/e2e-android.test.mjs` changed too. These are stand-in
helpers only, not an expectation, and each has its reason in a comment beside it:

- **`localeAnswers`** counts only `am get-config` as a reading. It counted any
  call that named "locale", from before run 6, when the script read
  `persist.sys.locale`; since then the script has read the language from `am
  get-config` alone, so for the old script nothing changes. The fix's `setprop
  persist.sys.locale nb-NO` would have been stamped and counted as a reading, and
  answered with a language, as no setprop does. A setprop now prints nothing, and a
  getprop answers without counting.
- **`timelinePhase` and `timelineAnswers`** treat a setprop as no question and
  print nothing, for the same reason: the `shell*locale*` pattern counted the
  fix's setprop as a question and answered it with the phase's property. The
  old script makes no setprop, so for it nothing changes.
- **`deviceAdbBody`'s `adb root`** now answers as adb does, and `adb
  wait-for-device` returns at once, on every device. `adb root` answered nothing
  before, which no adb does, so a script that judges it by what adb printed, as it
  must (adb exits 0 even when adbd refuses), would have been refused by the
  stand-in rather than by the device.
- **`runPastPreflight`** gains the options `switchable` and `rootAnswer`: additions
  only, no existing option changed.

`pnpm run tests:changes`, run by me on this checkout: "3 changed test file(s), none
weakened (RG-03)".

#### Evidence

- **Red `f7d4e2a`:** 17 failing tests and 2 guards, 399 passing. **Green
  `9caa720`.** The e2e-android, its lib and gate tests: 416 of 416 there, and
  **417 of 417 at the merge, `3d2255c`** (corrected 2026-10-23 from "416 of
  416"; one test came into `scripts/lib/e2e-android.test.mjs` in the pull

- **`gate:full`:** 11 steps passed. Its CI-01 step needs the session's proxy
  setting, and `gate:integrity` run alone with it gives 5 of 5. L3 needs Docker and
  L7 needs an emulator, so both run in CI.
- **`pnpm run req:coverage`,** run by me: "25 of 63 live requirements have a test
  that names them", the same figure as in the D-139 entry above. It wrote no change
  to `docs/requirements-status.md` (`git status` showed none), and it says
  REL-10-AC19 is not automated. I did not check which requirement IDs BUG-17's
  tests name.
- **Reviewers:** none is recorded here. `safety-reviewer`,
  `privacy-security-reviewer` and `test-auditor` are the blocking ones (D-043);
  whether they ran on this change is not in what I was given.

#### What changed in the records

- `docs/plan/decisions.md`: D-140 added. D-081's status line and
  `docs/specs/INF-06.md` (AC9, the wait for bokmål) each carry one line saying the
  `-change-locale` flow is superseded for CI by D-140; neither is rewritten.
- `docs/progress.md`: BUG-17's row now says fixed on the branch, not merged, with
  the evidence above; "In flight" says so and carries the 12:22:54 UTC rule;
  BUG-17 is out of the queued-bugs sentence. The header's date is 2026-10-22.
- **Two rows deleted, because their work has merged:** "GRP-01 follow-up" and
  "D-139", each of which said "Delete this row when it merges". #97 (`f3e682c`,
  merged 2026-10-21 07:29:35 UTC) removed the `.gitleaksignore` line, and #98
  (`a2457b5`, merged 2026-10-21 12:21:38 UTC) put the eleven sign-in files under a

- **Not changed:** `docs/plan/README.md` line 26 still says D-139's code owners are
  "on the session branch and not merged", which is stale since #98. M2's closing
  and BUG-63 are not recorded: the coordinating session records them after 12:25

  2026-10-23 as D-142, with BUG-63 as the ID; `progress/m2.md` has the story.)*

#### What is unverified here

- **That the switch works on CI's image** (API 37.2, `google_apis_ps16k`): that
  `adb root` and `setprop ctl.restart zygote` work there, and how long that restart
  takes. No emulator can run in a cloud session. The pull request's `android-e2e`
  run is the first proof, and until it has run, this fix is unproven.
- **That it removes the failure.** Three failures is a small number to judge a fix
  by, and the cause of the old failure is not known (above). The old setup also
  passed on re-run, so one green run on the pull request would not show that the
  failure is gone.
- **The job logs.** The sequence in jobs 118947039610 and 119070015215, the 130 s,
  and the job IDs are the coordinating session's report; I read no job log.
- **The counts and the gate.** 17 failing tests and 2 guards, 399 passing, 416 of

- **Window B's end,** 12:22:54 UTC on 2026-10-22, and the rule that nothing merges

- **Which emulator images allow `adb root`.** That a `google_apis` image does and a
  Play Store image does not is the coordinating session's statement; I checked no

- **What I did check in this checkout:** the diffs of `ci.yml`,
  `scripts/e2e-android.mjs`, `scripts/lib/e2e-android.mjs`,

### 2026-10-22 — M3 task 2 (GRP-02, CALL-01, CALL-02): specs and the owner's answers

**Where it stands, written so that it is true now.** *(Later the same day this
changed: step 1 was built, four reviewers passed it, the owner widened it three
times, and it was opened as #100. The last section of this entry, "Later the
same day", is the current state; the bullets below are as they stood when the
specs were finished.)*

- Task 2, the home screen (D-134), is **in progress: its three specs are written
  and the owner has answered every question (D-141).** No code and no test has
  been written for it. The next piece of work is its step 1, below.
- The specs are `docs/specs/GRP-02.md`, `CALL-01.md` and `CALL-02.md`. They were
  written on the recommended answers and now cite D-141 for them.
- Step 1 and task 2 have no pull request yet. Nothing in this entry has been run
  except what "What was checked" lists.

#### The questions and the answers

The three specs ask seven questions, Q1 to Q7. The owner was asked in session on
2026-10-22, each with Claude's recommendation, and **chose the recommendation
every time.** The full text is D-141.

| # | Question | The owner's answer |
|---|---|---|
| Q1 | Where #1 is kept | On the phone only, in secure storage. No server data about a possible non-member, and calling works when the server is down. A reinstall or a new phone loses #1. |
| Q2 | How #1 is entered | By typing a name and a number. No contacts permission, no new dependency. |
| Q3 | Is "112 one tap away" the dialer opening with 112 filled in? | Yes. Android's documentation says `ACTION_CALL` "cannot be used to call emergency numbers" and that applications "can dial emergency numbers using ACTION_DIAL, however". The walker presses call in the dialer, so the position is still sent automatically (AML). |
| Q4 | Which screens show 112 | Every screen the app shows, the join screen included: one rule in the shared screen component. Beyond the story's wording, which is why it was asked. |
| Q5 | Theme | Always dark for the MVP, one palette, contrast checked by a test. |
| Q6 | Minimum sizes | Call button at least 120 dp tall and full width; 112 at least 64 x 96 dp; other controls at least 56 dp; at least 24 dp between 112 and the call button. |
| Q7 | Code owner and safety filter for `features/call/` | Yes: `@bvst @urso-agent`, and the ai-review `safety` filter. Not a mutation path: the app's mutation testing comes with the safety core in task 3 (D-081 item 13). |

Two more answers, the same day:

- **Where task 2 is built:** "Her i skyen", in the cloud session, as GRP-01 was.
  Screens are tested with jest-expo and CI's `android-e2e` runs the flows. This
  amends D-134's "UI work runs on the Mac" for task 2 only. iOS and VoiceOver
  wait for task 12.

#### The owner's answers

**The first answer.** Claude asked in session (AskUserQuestion, with its
recommendation) whether `shared/ui/` should join step 1. **The owner chose the
recommendation: "Ja, hele shared/ui/"** (yes, the whole folder).

- `/apps/mobile/src/shared/ui/` gets the code owner `@bvst @urso-agent` and joins
  the `safety` filter, in the same step-1 pull request.
- Like `features/call/`, it is no D-139 named exception, no `SAFETY_PATHS` entry
  and no mutation group. Its tests are named CALL-02-AC10 (in
  `docs/specs/CALL-02.md`).
- **Claude's choices, under D-031,** offered in the same question: `phone.ts`
  checks for 110, 112 and 113 itself rather than relying on
  `features/grp/first-contact.ts`'s normalisation, so the emergency rule lives
  wholly in owned code; and a task-2 test requires every route under `src/app/`
  to be drawn through `Screen`.

**The second answer.** After `planner` and `safety-reviewer` raised it, Claude
asked (AskUserQuestion, with its recommendation) whether `apps/mobile/e2e/`, the
Maestro flows that run the app on Android in CI (L7), should join step 1 as
well. They had no owner. **The owner chose the recommendation: "Ja, ta med
e2e/"** (yes, include `e2e/`).

- The reasons, as the owner was asked:
  - the flows are the only end-to-end proof that 112 opens the dialer;
  - they carry the rule that a test never taps call in the dialer, so no test
    can ever really call 112 (SPIKE-01-AC10). On the emulator the dialer places no
    call by itself and the emulator has no network, but the rule matters more once
    the flows run on a real phone (L9);
  - an unowned change could drop the 112 checks or break that rule, and
    `safety-reviewer` would not be summoned by path; `test-auditor` would be the
    one to see it.
- `/apps/mobile/e2e/` gets the code owner `@bvst @urso-agent` and joins the
  `safety` filter, in the same step-1 pull request. Like the other two folders,
  it is no D-139 named exception, no `SAFETY_PATHS` entry and no mutation group.
- Its tests are named CALL-02-AC11 (in `docs/specs/CALL-02.md`). **The folder
  exists today** (`app-starts.yaml`, `first-admin-invites.yaml` and
  `wrong-code.yaml`, all tracked), so step 1 pins its tracked files itself. For
  the other two folders that pin waits for task 2.
- **The cost:** every later change to an L7 flow waits for the owner and brings
  `safety-reviewer`. It is small, because task pull requests already need the
  owner through D-139's paths (#96 did; task 2's does).
- **Not read here:** the three flows against SPIKE-01-AC10's rule.

**The third answer.** After `safety-reviewer`'s delta review of #100 (PASS on
`18007c2`) raised it, Claude asked (AskUserQuestion, with its recommendation)
whether `apps/mobile/src/app/` should join step 1. It holds Expo Router's routes:
`_layout.tsx`, the frame around every screen, and `index.tsx`, both tracked. It
had no owner. A change there could draw something over the 112 button (a modal, a
splash) or show an error screen without 112, and only the advisory UI reviewer
would see it. **The owner chose the recommendation: "Ja, ta med src/app/"** (yes,
include `src/app/`).

- `/apps/mobile/src/app/` gets the code owner `@bvst @urso-agent` and joins the
  `safety` filter, in step 1's pull request, #100. Like the others, it is no D-139
  named exception, no `SAFETY_PATHS` entry and no mutation group.
- Its tests are named CALL-02-AC12 (the spec is being written). **The folder
  exists today,** so step 1 pins its tracked files itself, as for `e2e/`.
- **Claude's four choices, under D-031,** offered in the same question, from
  `safety-reviewer`'s delta should-fixes. `planner` is writing them into the specs;
  D-141 lists them under "Who decided" as (c) to (f):
  - (c) `_layout.tsx` exports an `ErrorBoundary` whose fallback is drawn on
    `Screen`, so a crash still shows 112;
  - (d) an ESLint rule lets `Modal`, `SafeAreaView` and `ScrollView` be imported
    only inside `shared/ui/`;
  - (e) `phone.ts` removes only its own separators; anything that is not then
    digits, with an optional leading `+`, goes to the dialer; and it builds the
    `tel:` URI from the cleaned string;
  - (f) the emergency set becomes 112, 911, 110, 113, 000, 08, 118, 119 and 999,
    and any of these followed by more digits also goes to the dialer.
- **The cost, stated plainly:** every later change to a route waits for the owner
  and brings `safety-reviewer`. A task's pull request that adds a route, as a new
  screen usually does, now waits even if nothing else in it is owner-gated. And
  (f) sends any number that starts with one of the nine, such as one starting
  `08`, to the dialer: a second tap, not a failed call. The owner was not asked
  about that separately.
- **Not in #100 yet:** `src/app/` has no red or green commit on the side branch.

**What changed in the records:** D-141 item 7 carries a pointer, "Who decided"
has the six choices, (a) to (f), and the entry ends with "Amended 2026-10-22",
which holds the three answers. The specs' changes (CALL-02-AC10, CALL-02-AC11 and
the amendments in `CALL-01.md`, `CALL-02.md` and `GRP-02.md`) are `planner`'s,
made in parallel; this entry did not edit or review them, and `CALL-02.md`, as
read in this worktree, still says `src/app/` stays unowned (its "Modules and
files affected", near its end), which the third answer reverses.
`progress.md`'s header, task-2 row, #95 paragraph and "In flight" follow the
decisions, and its paragraph about window B is corrected: the window ended, so
the ban on merging is over.

    files (five nested `afterEach` blocks in all). Expo Router's `renderRouter` turns on fake timers,
    and CALL-02-AC7 puts React Native's StatusBar in the root layout. A nested
    `afterEach` switched to real timers before the outer `cleanup()` unmounted
    StatusBar, so StatusBar passed its fake immediate id to Node's real
    `clearImmediate`. That corrupted Node's immediate count: React's `act()`
    never resolved, and later launches hung until the 90 s timeout. Now each
    file's top-level `afterEach` brings real timers back after `cleanup()`.
    `test-auditor` ran the old `grp.test.tsx` against the new code: it hung for
    more than 600 s. The new one passes 103 of 103.

#### The owner's answer on the 112 label (D-141, fourth amendment)

`a11y-i18n-reviewer` flagged that a screen reader likely reads "Ring 112,
nødnummeret" as "ring hundre og tolv", where Norwegians say "én én to". Its note
says it reasoned this: it ran no TalkBack and no Norwegian text-to-speech voice.

Claude asked the owner, with a recommendation, whether the screen-reader text
should be "Ring 1 1 2, nødnummeret", read digit by digit, with the visible text
staying "112". **The owner answered "Ja, «Ring 1 1 2»" on 2026-10-22,** the
recommendation. So:
- CALL-02-AC6's label is "Ring 1 1 2, nødnummeret", and the hint "Åpner
  telefonappen med 1 1 2. Trykk ring der.";
- the English is "Call 1 1 2, the emergency number" and "Opens your phone app
  with 1 1 2. Tap call there.";
- the visible text stays "112".

**Not verified:** how TalkBack reads either form. Only a real phone (L9) shows
it. The failure line, "Telefonen kunne ikke åpne 112. Ring 112 selv.", was not
part of the question and is unchanged. The English hint changes by one more
word than the digits: `en.json` has "your phone app" today.

#### Review loop 1

From the five reviews of `cff3021`; the changes, as `e128741`'s message gives
them:
- **The owner's 112 answer:** label, hint and the failure line's label spell
  "1 1 2"; the visible text is unchanged.
- **Safety:** `phone.ts` treats a rejected permission read as not granted, so the
  dialer opens and nothing rejects. A 403, 404 or 409 on `GET /v1/me` keeps the
  credential and #1 (only a 401 signs out). ESLint allows `expo-intent-launcher`,
  `expo-linking`, `PermissionsAndroid` and `Linking` only in `features/call/phone.ts`
  (CALL-01-AC6). The 112 button has its own test ID, and `emergency-112.yaml`
  waits for it to go before it asserts 112 in the dialer, so the L7 flow can no
  longer pass on the app's own 112 button.
- **Privacy:** a join the server accepts deletes #1, best effort, before it
  saves the new credential.
- **Accessibility and language:** `expo-system-ui` ~57.0.4, so that always-dark
  applies on Android (a new dependency, D-031; `apps/mobile/package.json` is an
  owner path); the call button's name carries its line; "Lagre" dismisses the
  keyboard; a new view scrolls to the top and focus goes to its heading
  (GRP-02-AC11, new).
- **Scripts:** a scripted flow that is absent or untagged stops the run, naming
  it; `KEYCODE_BACK` only after the window check; `engines.node` and `doctor`
  both name Node 22.18.

**`4989261`: GRP-01-AC30's filter was too wide.** The loop-1 green made the
explaining call button read "Velg hvem du vil ringe. Du har ikke valgt en
nummer 1-kontakt. Trykk for å velge." GRP-01-AC30's filter, `/prøv|try/i`,
matched "try" inside "Trykk" and took the button for a retry control. It now
matches the retry words as whole words only. AC30's intent is unchanged: every
control that offers to try again is named "Prøv igjen" (RG-03; the reason is
beside it in the test).

**`a54f735`: an order-dependent jest failure in `phone.test.tsx`.** The
coordinating session saw 13 tests fail in 2 or 3 of about 11 full runs, and never
when the file ran alone.
- **Root cause.** Jest's per-worker resolver cache keys module IDs by (requiring
  file, name). `app.test.tsx` and `grp.test.tsx` load the real `phone.ts`, so the
  resolver caches `phone.ts`'s `require` of `expo-intent-launcher` as the real
  path. The `{ virtual: true }` mock of the installed package, keyed by the bare
  name, was then skipped later in the same worker, and `phone.test.tsx` met the
  real module.
- **Proof.** In one worker, `app.test.tsx` then `phone.test.tsx` gave 13 failed
  before the fix and 208 passed after (test-auditor's note,
  `.claude/agent-memory/test-auditor/feedback_jest_virtual_mock_cache.md`).
- **Fix.** The mock is no longer virtual: the same mock and factory, keyed by the
  package's real path. No assertion changed, nothing was skipped. The failure
  was red (the real module ran), never a false green, and the fix weakens
  nothing (`safety-reviewer`'s note).
- **A correction to `e128741`'s message.** It reports "13 failures in one suite"
  in a run made while another agent ran jest, with the cause not identified. The
  cause was this one, not the other agent.

**Loop 1's delta reviews.** `safety-reviewer`, `privacy-security-reviewer` and
`a11y-i18n-reviewer` passed `d2a55ca` (notes: `eb66de3`). `test-auditor` passed
`c827f21`, `d2a55ca` plus the loop-2 spec text (notes: `37e8b0d`). With
`code-reviewer`, that is all five PASS, as the coordinating session passed it on.
**`code-reviewer`'s own verdicts for either loop are not in a committed note**
(its memory folder last changed in `0581abe`); I saw its work only as "note 9" in
GRP-02's loop-2 table.

From loop 1's delta reviews. The specs changed first (`c827f21`, `37e8b0d`,
`06efdd9`).
- **The mock guard (`4c38bb2`, test-only).** `{ virtual: true }` is dropped from

  (`app.test.tsx`, `grp.test.tsx`, `home.test.tsx`, `call.test.tsx`,

  refuses a `{ virtual: true }` mock of a bare name that resolves from the file
  holding it, in any code file under `apps/mobile`. Before the change it failed,
  naming the 12 mocks; after, it passes. The commit message gives five orders and
  a parallel run of the mobile suite, each green.
- **Red `f3be1e1`, green `9c7c498`:**
  - **#1 is deleted at a start with no credential,** best effort and silently
    (`privacy-security-reviewer` S1). A phone whose 401 sign-out saw the store
    refuse #1's delete, and that never joins again, kept the name and number
    under the join screen for as long as the app stayed installed, where the
    walker cannot see them. A refusal does not stop the join screen, and the
    delete is tried again at every start. A credential read that rejects deletes
    nothing. GRP-02-AC6 and AC9.
  - **The call hint only says "Ringer" while the permission reads granted;** in
    every other state it says the permission is missing (CALL-01-AC4, AC8). The
    visible text is unchanged.
  - **The calling-imports rule is wider** (CALL-01-AC6). Outside `phone.ts`,
    ESLint also refuses deep paths into `expo-intent-launcher`, `expo-linking`,
    `expo-modules-core` and `react-native`; `NativeModules`,
    `TurboModuleRegistry`, `requireNativeModule` and `requireOptionalNativeModule`;
    `import()` of any of those modules, a variable source included; and a namespace
    or default import of `react-native`, `expo` and `expo-modules-core`.
  - **The dead `absent` field is gone** from `scriptedFlowsToRun`'s result and its
    two tests (`code-reviewer`; RG-03: `toEqual` still holds the whole result).
- **The `expo/build` deep-path gap** is recorded in CALL-01's Risks (`91d0bce`).
  `safety-reviewer` N4 and `privacy-security-reviewer` N-A: `expo/build/Expo`

| `72ad66d` | `gate:quick` | `3 passed` |
| `72ad66d` | mobile jest | `706 passed` |
| `72ad66d` | stability | `test-auditor` ran the full mobile suite in several orders in one worker, all green (`705 passed`, before the test `72ad66d` added) |
| `72ad66d` | `req:coverage` (mine) | 28 of 63 live requirements have a test that names them; GRP-02 9, CALL-01 18, CALL-02 18, all 🟢; the committed report is unchanged by the run |

- **Why CI-01 runs on its own.** A session reaches GitHub through a proxy, and
  `gate:integrity` needs `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=1` to use it (3 of 5 without it, as
  the entry above records; 5 of 5 with it, which I read again). Setting the variable for the whole `gate:full` breaks
  `scripts/lib/proc.test.mjs`, because every child process then prints Node's
  `UNDICI-EHPA` warning, which ends up in the output the tests compare. I ran that
  file on `72ad66d`: **3 failed and 5 passed with the variable, 8 of 8 without.**
  So `gate:full` runs without it and `gate:integrity` runs apart, with it. It is
  now a line under "Live gotchas" in `progress.md`.
- **The mutation budget.** The `d2a55ca` failure is about the budget, not the
  tests: `gate:full` runs the nine groups one after another inside 25 minutes,
  and that run used them up on seven. The two that did not finish passed when
  run alone with the local `--group` that D-129 added. I did not read that run's
  log; this is the coordinating session's account.
- **Not run here: L3 and L7.** The integration tests need Docker; the Maestro
  flows need an emulator. **CI's `android-e2e` is the first time the flows run,**
  and the first observation of "the dialer opens with 112 filled in" (D-141 "Not
  verified when this was decided").

#### Not verified

- That the dialer opens with 112 filled in on Android (D-141): CI's `android-e2e`.
- How TalkBack reads "Ring 1 1 2, nødnummeret" or its hint: only a real phone (L9).
- Anything on iOS, and VoiceOver: task 12.
- That `android-e2e` passes. *Added after the PR opened (#101):* its first
  run, on `96c04b7` (run 39956809738, job 120424570385), failed. Its job log
  shows `first-admin-invites`, `first-contact` and `call-with-permission`
  passing ("one outgoing call, placed by no.trygghverdag.placeholder with no
  second tap, to #1's number": CALL-01-AC2's first observation), then
  `call-without-permission` failing at its first wait (`Assertion is false:
  "Knappen åpner telefonappen, og du må trykke ring der\." is visible`, after
  30 s), right after the script pressed `KEYCODE_ENDCALL`. The flows after it,
  `emergency-112` among them, did not run. The cause was inferred, not seen
  (the end-call key with no call in progress puts the phone to sleep); the fix
  is CALL-01 approach 9's wake step, end-call guard and failure evidence.
  **Corrected 2026-10-25: the cause is confirmed,** by the run on `16c46f3` (job
  120473565944): the call to #1 had already ended before the script read
  Telecom, so it pressed no key, and every flow passed. That run is also the first
  observation on a device of CALL-01-AC2, CALL-01-AC4 and CALL-02-AC3 (the dialer
  with 112 in front, no new call). The entry of 2026-10-25 has the lines and what
  they do and do not show.

#### For the owner

- **Approve task 2's pull request** when it is open (D-042). It touches owner
  paths, among them `apps/mobile/package.json` (the new `expo-system-ui`),
  `secure-storage.ts`, `session.tsx` and `packages/config/` (D-141
  "Consequences"), and, from step 1, `features/call/`, `shared/ui/`, `e2e/` and
  `src/app/`.
- **WCAG 2.5.3 (Label in Name), answered: kept.** The 112 button's
  accessible name, "Ring 1 1 2, nødnummeret", does not contain the visible "112",
  so a Voice Access user who says "tap 112" may not reach it. The label is the
  owner's own answer (D-141, fourth amendment), so changing it is the owner's.
  `a11y-i18n-reviewer` raised it; CALL-02's spec records it as "not changed". The
  coordinating session asked the owner on 2026-10-23, with two options: keep it
  (recommended: TalkBack reads "1 1 2" digit by digit, and Voice Access can still
  reach the button with "show numbers"), or put "112" first (Voice Access matches
  it, but some voices may read it as "hundred and twelve", and it would need a
  third loop). The answer: "Behold som nå", keep it. No change to D-141, since
  the label is unchanged; a trade-off to look at again with real users. (Added
  by the coordinating session after this entry was written.)

#### Follow-ups, queued and not done

1. **The `expo/build` / `expo/src` deep-path pattern and its probe** (CALL-01
   Risks). `expo` is not in `CALLING_PACKAGES`.
2. **The start's secure-store calls have no time limit** (`safety-reviewer`, loop
   2, its note N1 as the coordinating session numbered it). Expo's Android
   `deleteValueWithKeyAsync` always settles today (a synchronous
   `SharedPreferences` removal that resolves or throws), so the waiting view
   always ends. A time limit in `shared/secure-storage.ts` would be the fix.
3. **On iOS, Keychain items, the credential included, can outlive an uninstall**
   (`privacy-security-reviewer`, N-C as the coordinating session numbered it; I
   did not find it written in a committed note). That belongs in task 12's spec.
4. **WCAG 2.5.3**, above: answered, kept; look at it again with real users.

   the stale `timelinePhase` docblock in `scripts/e2e-android.test.mjs` and the

6. **The guard's obscure spellings** (`{ ['virtual']: true }`, `jest['mock']`, an

#### `android-e2e` on #101: two failures, the cause inferred, then confirmed

- **Run 39956809738 on `96c04b7`** (job 120424570385, 12:53:50 to 13:06:29 UTC)
  failed in the step "Boot the emulator, switch it to bokmål and run every flow"
  (13:00:45 to 13:06:27). From the log, as the coordinating session read it
  (comment of 13:10 UTC): `first-admin-invites`, `first-contact` and
  `call-with-permission` passed; the script then pressed `KEYCODE_ENDCALL`; and
  `call-without-permission.yaml` failed at its first wait (`Assertion is false:
  "Knappen åpner telefonappen, og du må trykke ring der\." is visible`, after
  30 s). The comment called the cause "inferred, not seen".
- **Run 39959347972 on `2cc97d9`** (job 120433100132, 13:12:55 to 13:26:24 UTC)
  failed in the same step (13:20:22 to 13:26:18). That push held specs only, so
  the code was the first run's. That it failed at the same flow is the
  coordinating session's reading; the API shows only the step.
- **The fix,** CALL-01 approach 9 (`gh api` for the commits): red `2ad0ce6`
  (13:56 UTC), green `0acf098` (14:25 UTC), then `16c46f3` (requirement status,
  14:36 UTC). The script checks that the phone is awake before each scripted flow
  and wakes it if not, presses the end-call key only while Telecom still shows the
  call in progress, and prints the wake state and the app in front when a flow
  fails.
- **Run 39971068311 on `16c46f3`** (job 120473565944, 14:36:28 to 14:50:47 UTC)
  succeeded; the step ran 14:44:15 to 14:50:44, and the two failure-only steps were
  skipped. From the log, as posted in the coordinating session's comment of
  14:52 UTC:
  - `call-with-permission.yaml: the new call had already ended (Telecom's events
    for it: CREATED, SET_CONNECTING, AUDIO_ROUTE, START_CONNECTION,
    CAPABILITY_CHANGE, SET_DIALING, SET_DISCONNECTED, LOG_CALL,
    CONF_CALLS_CHANGED, CONF_CALLS_CHANGED, DESTROYED). No key pressed.`;
  - no wake line was printed for any flow;
  - every flow passed: "every flow passed: the first run, and 7 on their own".
- **What this confirms, and what it does not.** The emulator's call to #1 ends by
  itself before the script reads Telecom, so the new script presses no key, where
  the old one pressed `KEYCODE_ENDCALL` unconditionally, with no call in progress.
  With the press gone, no flow needed a wake and every flow passed. So the press
  was the cause of the first two failures: the same flows fail with it and pass
  without it. **It does not show that the phone went to sleep.** No run printed
  the phone's state (the first comment says the debug artifact cannot be read from
  a session), and the wake step never fired in the passing run. "The end-call key
  with no call in progress puts the phone to sleep" is still an explanation, not
  an observation.
- **First observations on a device** (the same comment): CALL-01-AC2 ("one
  outgoing call, placed by no.trygghverdag.placeholder with no second tap, to #1's
  number"); CALL-01-AC4 (`call-without-permission`: the dialer in front, and no new
  call); CALL-02-AC3 (`emergency-112`: "the dialer, com.google.android.dialer, is
  in front", and "Telecom's record shows no new call"). `back-from-the-dialer` and
  `large-text` passed too.

#### BUG-64: the dependency audit on `main`

- **The failure.** `security` (job 121181676976) concluded `failure` at 13:26:32
  UTC; its one annotation is "Process completed with exit code 1". The
  coordinating session read the log: "21 vulnerabilities found · Severity: 2 low |
  15 moderate | 4 high (1 ignored)". **Reproduced:** `pnpm audit --audit-level
  high` (pnpm 10.33.0) on a `git archive` of `05bfb73` in a scratch folder printed
  that summary line, and two high advisories with these paths:
  - GHSA-mrhx-c7xc-m3w5, "Zod can exhaust memory while validating oversized
    constrained collections": vulnerable `<4.6.6`, patched `>=4.6.6`;
    `apps__server>zod` (locked 4.6.5) and `apps__mobile>expo>@expo/cli>zod`
    (3.25.76);
  - GHSA-xjj6-f33q-929c, "undici vulnerable to Denial of Service via unhandled
    error on excessive content-encodings": vulnerable `>=8.0.0 <8.12.2`, patched
    `>=8.12.2`; `apps__server>@testcontainers/postgresql>testcontainers>undici`
    (8.11.0), a dev dependency.
- **Not #101's.** `security` passed on `16c46f3` at 14:36:51 UTC on 2026-10-23.
  #101's lockfile change is two app packages, `expo-intent-launcher` and
  `expo-system-ui` (and its root `package.json` change is `engines.node`): no zod,
  no undici. The same audit on a `git archive` of `50214c0`, before #101, gives
  the same two advisories and the same summary line today. So what changed is
  npm's audit data. **On the dates:** I was told the advisories were published
  after the green run. `pnpm audit --json` (2026-10-25) shows GHSA-xjj6-f33q-929c
  created 22:14:31 UTC on 2026-10-23, which is after it. GHSA-mrhx-c7xc-m3w5 shows
  created 2026-10-01 and updated 18:26:52 UTC on 2026-10-23, also after it, so it
  was changed after the green run, not first published. The data does not say what
  the change was.
- **What the fix can reach** (`pnpm view`, 2026-10-25): zod's `latest` is 4.6.7
  (4.6.6 exists), undici's is 8.12.3. `testcontainers` 12.1.0, the locked one,
  declares `undici ^8.9.0`, and its newest, 12.2.1, `^8.11.2`. `@expo/cli` 58.1.15 is
  `latest` (published 23:27 UTC on 2026-10-23, npm's time field) and declares
  `zod ^3.25.76`, as does the locked 57.0.27; the advisory's patched range starts
  at 4.6.6, so no 3.x is patched. Besides the server and the contracts, two more
  locked zod 4.6.5: `@orpc/zod` 1.15.3 (peer `>=3.25.0`) and
  `mutation-server-protocol` 0.4.1 (`zod ^4.1.12`, under `@stryker-mutator/core`),
  which `test-author`'s lockfile test found.
- **What `@expo/cli` does with zod** (read in the installed 57.0.27; 58.1.15 was
  not installed and not read). `require("zod")` appears in two files of `build/`,
  both under `src/start/server/`: `DevToolsPlugin.schema.js` (the shape of a
  dev-tools plugin's descriptor, with arrays, one with `.min(1)`, and of the
  output lines a plugin's extension prints) and `createMCPDevToolsExtensionSchema.js`
  (a flat object of an enum, numbers, booleans and strings, no collection). The
  descriptors come from autolinking's scan of the packages installed in the
  project for their `devtools` platform. **That is not what the question said.**
  It described the tool as using zod "when the app is built"; the code I read is
  the development server's. Both are developer tooling reading what is installed
  here, so I judge the risk the same kind, but I did not trace whether
  `expo prebuild`, `expo export` or the release build load those files at all.
  D-143 records both descriptions.
- **The owner's question and answer.** Asked in session, in Norwegian, on
  2026-10-25. It named the advisory, which is new on `main` and not from #101; that
  the server's zod and undici can be updated; and that Expo's tool, even 58.1.15,
  uses zod 3.25.76 with no fixed 3.x, used only when the app is built, reading only
  our own configuration files. Two options, in the coordinating session's words:
  - **"Godta den, registrert (Recommended)":** BUG-64 updates the server's zod to
    4.6.6 or later and undici to 8.12.2 or later, and accepts GHSA-mrhx-c7xc-m3w5
    only for the Expo tool's path, like D-093. A test ensures the server and the
    contracts always use zod 4.6.6 or later, and the acceptance is reconsidered
    when Expo moves to zod 4;
  - **"Tving zod 4 inn i Expo":** override `@expo/cli`'s zod to 4.6.6. No
    exception, but zod 4 is not fully compatible with zod 3, so Expo's tool could
    break the app's build, visible only in CI's `android-e2e`.

  The owner answered **"Godta den, registrert (Recommended)"**. Recorded as
  **D-143**, in the form of D-093 and D-104.

### 2026-10-26 — BUG-65 and BUG-66, fixed in #105 (not merged): the audit-settings check reads its files as pnpm does, and the root `.npmrc` may name no registry
