`scripts/dependency-audit.test.mjs` are fixed on the branch of
[#105](https://github.com/bvst/TryggHverdag/pull/105): BUG-65, a hole in BUG-11's check
that nothing but the root `package.json` sets pnpm's audit settings, and BUG-66, a
setting no check read, the registry. Both are test-only; the one file is
`scripts/dependency-audit.test.mjs`. **#105 is not merged and these commits are not
yet pushed:** on `main`, both holes are open until it merges. D-144, the code owner for
`/pnpm-workspace.yaml` and `/pnpm-lock.yaml` that the owner answered yes to earlier
today (below), is still unwritten and follows #105 in its own pull request.
The next free IDs are **BUG-67** and **D-144**.
*Corrected the same day, later: the commits above were pushed (`4577ecf` is the head
on `origin`), and CI and all five reviewers passed `4577ecf`, as the coordinating
session reported; I opened no check. Two of the reviewers found a third bug in the
same file, BUG-67, which is fixed in #105 too (the entry below). The next free IDs
are now **BUG-68** and **D-144**.*

**What I checked, and what I was told.** On the branch at `c37d0f0`, on 2026-10-26, I
ran `scripts/dependency-audit.test.mjs` (41 of 41 passed) and `pnpm run tests:changes`
("1 changed test file(s), none weakened (RG-03)"). I read `.github/CODEOWNERS` (no line

#### The owner's answer on the pnpm files' code owner

The entry above lists the question as asked and not answered. **It was answered the same
day,** in session: the owner chose "Ja, begge filene (Recommended)", so `/pnpm-workspace.yaml`
and `/pnpm-lock.yaml` both get a code owner. That is as `progress.md` records it; I was not

written. Until it merges, a change to either file has no owner. BUG-65's check in #105
refuses an audit setting in the workspace file, but it is a test, not an owner: other

*Corrected later the same day: D-144 is not "its own small pull request after #105". After
`privacy-security-reviewer`'s BLOCK (BUG-69) the owner chose to put it into #105, with four
more paths; it is written (the BUG-69 entry at the end of this file).*

#### BUG-65: the check read text, and pnpm reads keys

- **The bug.** BUG-11's test ("nothing else sets pnpm's audit settings or loads a hook
  that could…") matched the raw text of `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, the root `.npmrc` and the
  workflows against the words that set audit settings or load a hook (`pnpmfile`,
  `auditConfig`, `ignoreGhsas`, `ignoreCves`, `configDependencies`). pnpm does not read
  those files as text. It decodes them, so a name written with escapes, or filled in
  from the environment, is the setting to pnpm and nothing to the test. Found by
  `privacy-security-reviewer` on #105.
- **The routes, shown end to end with pnpm 10.33.0.** `pnpm audit --audit-level high`
  exits 0 with "2 high (2 ignored)"; the control, with no ignore anywhere, exits 1.
  `test-author` ran all four in a scratch copy:
  - (a) workspace keys written with unicode escapes,
    `"audit\u0043onfig": {"ignore\u0047hsas": [...]}`. **The coordinating session
    re-ran this one itself:** exit 0, against the control's exit 1.
  - (b) a workspace key pnpm fills in from the environment,
    `"pnpm${TRYGG_UNSET-f}ile": hooks.cjs`. pnpm runs `envReplace` over top-level keys,
    and `${NAME-fallback}` gives the fallback when NAME is unset.
  - (c) a quoted, JSON-escaped pnpmfile name in `.npmrc`: pnpm's `ini` 1.3.8 JSON-parses
    quoted names.
  - (d) an environment-filled pnpmfile name in `.npmrc`.
  - **The workflow case** (escaped YAML in `.github/workflows`) was read in pnpm's
    bundle and **not run**: workflows are code-owned. *Corrected the same day, later:*
    `test-author`'s reads of "pnpm's bundle" for BUG-65 were of pnpm **10.28.0**'s, not
    10.33.0's (see the note under "The parser", below). The re-check against 10.33.0's
    bundle (YAML reader, settings fill-in, `.npmrc` reader, every YAML fixture) was not
    reported to name this workflow read, so it is **unverified against 10.33.0**.
    Routes (a) to (d) used the `pnpm` command and stand.
- **The fix** (red `5620397`, green `96c7156`, before the squash):
  - the workspace file is parsed as YAML. Every scalar at every depth, key or value, is
    decoded and checked against the route pattern. Any key containing `${` is refused. A
    parse error or a warning is refused, never read as clean;
  - `.npmrc` names are decoded by a port of `ini` 1.3.8's `decode` and `unsafe`, and
    checked the same way;
  - workflow scalars are decoded, and any audit other than `pnpm audit --audit-level
    high` is refused, a folded `run: >` included (`pnpm` and `audit` on lines of their own
    are read as one command);
  - the raw-text checks stay. No existing assertion changed: `tests:changes` says "1
    changed test file(s), none weakened (RG-03)" (I ran it).
- **The parser.** `yaml` 2.9.1, which the lockfile resolves as the root vite's peer. It
  is loaded with `createRequire` from vite's folder, the pattern `stryker-config.test.mjs`
  uses. No dependency or lockfile change. If the peer disappears, the tests fail with a
  named error ("BUG-65: no YAML parser resolves through the root's vite…"), not quietly.
  pnpm's own parser is `@zkochan/js-yaml` 0.0.9, bundled in pnpm and not loadable from a
  test. `test-author` ran every fixture through both, and every route word pnpm decodes,
  `yaml` decodes too. The two differences the test's header records, a `<<` merge and an
  unknown tag, are read where written or refused.
  *Corrected the same day, later: the version is wrong.* pnpm 10.33.0's YAML reader is
  `@zkochan/js-yaml` **0.0.11**. 0.0.9 is the bundle of pnpm **10.28.0**, which
  `/opt/node-tools` holds (its launcher hands over to the 10.33.0 that `packageManager`
  names), and `test-author` read that bundle while saying "10.33.0". So "every fixture
  through both" was run against 0.0.9. It was run again against 10.33.0's bundle for
  BUG-67's review: the fixtures' readings held, and the differences are not only the two
  named here. The test's header now also records a lone CR (a line break to pnpm's reader,
  a character to `yaml`) and an undefined alias `*x` (an error to pnpm's reader, not to
  `yaml`'s `parseDocument`). The end-to-end runs above used the `pnpm` command, which is
  10.33.0, and stand. The entry of the same day below has the BLOCK that found it.
- **Not pinned** (listed in the test's header): a command a workflow builds at run time,
  such as shell quoting (`pnpm "au"dit`), `${{ }}` expressions and repository variables;
  where `yaml` and pnpm's parser differ beyond the fixtures; pnpm settings outside the
  repository.

#### BUG-66: a `registry=` line redirects the audit

- **The bug.** A `registry=` line in the root `.npmrc` makes `pnpm audit` ask that
  server for advisories. `.npmrc` has no code owner, and no check read the setting:
  BUG-11's words and BUG-65's decoded names are about hooks and audit settings, and
  `registry` is neither.
- **Found and reproduced** by the coordinating session on 2026-10-26, while verifying
  BUG-65, in a scratch copy with pnpm 10.33.0. A local server answering every request
  with zero vulnerabilities (it logged `POST /-/npm/v1/security/audits/quick`) made
  `pnpm audit --audit-level high` exit 0, "No known vulnerabilities found". The
  unchanged `.npmrc` exits 1, "2 high".
- **What did not redirect the audit** (exit 1, real advisories): a `registry:` key in
  `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, and `registry=` in `apps/mobile/.npmrc`.
  *Corrected the same day* (`privacy-security-reviewer` and `test-auditor` on #105, at
  `4577ecf`): **the first half is true of a plain `registry:` key only, and it must not
  be read as "the workspace file cannot redirect the audit". A `registries:` key in
  `pnpm-workspace.yaml` does** (BUG-67, the entry below), and the list in #105 (not
  merged) refuses it. The `apps/mobile/.npmrc` fact stands.*
- **In CI.** The `security` job runs `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` and then `pnpm
  audit --audit-level high` (`ci.yml`, lines 786 and 788), and both read the root
  `.npmrc`. So an attacker would need a pass-through registry that serves the real
  packages and reports no advisories. That is feasible. Today's root `.npmrc` is one
  line, `engine-strict=true`.
- **The fix** (red `6e96a4c`, green `c37d0f0`, before the squash). The root `.npmrc` may
  hold only the settings in `RC_SETTINGS`, today just `engine-strict`. Every decoded
  name is checked, section names included. Adding a setting is a change to this test,
  and `/scripts/` is code-owned, so the owner approves it. Controls refused: registry,
  scoped registry, an auth line, `registry[]`, escaped and quoted registry names,
  proxy, https-proxy, strict-ssl, cafile, noproxy, and a `[registry]` section.
- **Not tried:** proxy, `strict-ssl` and the certificate settings, as routes. The list
  refuses them either way.
- **Not asked.** `.npmrc` itself still has no CODEOWNERS entry, and the owner has not
  been asked about one. The owner's D-144 answer names only `pnpm-workspace.yaml` and
  `pnpm-lock.yaml`. Queued as a follow-up, not an open question (`progress.md`, "In
  flight", item 14). *Answered later the same day, after the BLOCK on #105: yes, in D-144
  (the BUG-69 entry at the end of this file).*

### 2026-10-26 — BUG-67, fixed in #105 (not merged): a `registries:` key in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` redirects the audit, and the workspace file may now hold only `packages` and `catalog`

**Where it stands, written so that it is true now.** BUG-67 was found by
`privacy-security-reviewer` and `test-auditor`, each reviewing
[#105](https://github.com/bvst/TryggHverdag/pull/105) at `4577ecf` (the records commit of
the entry above), and it is fixed in #105. It is test-only; the one file is
`scripts/dependency-audit.test.mjs`. The pre-squash commits are `dac3614` (red) and
`bc5a6f9` (green), and `62c2ce2` holds `code-reviewer`'s two tidy-ups. **#105 is not merged,
and these three commits are not pushed as I write this:** I fetched the branch on
2026-10-26, `origin`'s head is `4577ecf`, and `git log origin/claude/busy-faraday-40n2zl..HEAD`
lists exactly these three. On `main` the hole is open until #105 merges. D-144, the code
owner for `/pnpm-workspace.yaml` and `/pnpm-lock.yaml`, still follows #105 in its own pull
request; the owner's answer is unchanged. The next free IDs are **BUG-68** and **D-144**.
*Corrected 2026-10-26, after the BLOCK on `ec81f65`: D-144 is not a pull request after #105; it
is in #105 (the BUG-69 entry at the end of this file).*
*Corrected the same day, later: `892d7c9` (the records) and `82c4be0` (test follow-ups) are
on #105 too, and BUG-68 is taken (the entry below). The next free IDs are now **BUG-69**
and **D-144**.*

**What I checked, and what I was told.** On the branch at `62c2ce2`, on 2026-10-26, I ran
`scripts/dependency-audit.test.mjs` (44 of 44 passed) and `pnpm run tests:changes` ("1
changed test file(s), none weakened (RG-03)"). I read the test file's `WORKSPACE_SETTINGS`
(`new Set(['packages', 'catalog'])`), its `workspaceUnlisted` and the three tests whose
names begin `BUG-67:`, and I checked that `decisions.md` has no D-144 and that BUG-68 is
named nowhere in the tracked files. Everything else is what `test-author`, the two
reviewers and the coordinating session reported, named where it matters. I did not repeat
the scratch-copy runs of pnpm, I opened no job log, and I did not run `gate:quick`: the
coordinating session runs it before the push.

#### Found by two reviewers, with the same should-fix

- `privacy-security-reviewer` and `test-auditor` each gave a PASS on #105 at `4577ecf`, and
  each listed this as a should-fix: a `registries:` key in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` redirects
  the audit.
- `test-auditor` read the path in pnpm's bundle: the workspace file's keys are copied onto
  the config, and the audit uses `opts.registries.default`. `privacy-security-reviewer` ran
  it end to end.
- `privacy-security-reviewer` also saw that the BUG-66 records said only that a `registry:`
  key in the workspace file did not redirect the audit, which is true but reads as "the
  file cannot". Both records are corrected in place, marked *Corrected the same day*: the
  BUG-66 row in `progress.md`, and the BUG-66 section of the entry above.
- `code-reviewer` gave a PASS with two should-fixes, both tidy-ups in the test file (below).

#### The bug

- **pnpm 10.33.0 copies `pnpm-workspace.yaml`'s top-level keys into its settings, with no
  check on the key.** The audit sends its request to `registries.default`, so a
  `registries:` key in that file redirects it. A plain `registry:` key does not. No check
  read the file for this, and the file has no code owner until D-144 lands.
- **Reproduced by the coordinating session** on 2026-10-26, in a scratch copy with pnpm
  10.33.0. Today's workspace file plus a `registries:` key whose `default:` points at a local
  server that reports zero vulnerabilities makes `pnpm audit --audit-level high` exit 0, "No
  known vulnerabilities found". The server logged `POST /-/npm/v1/security/audits/quick`. The
  control, today's file unchanged, exits 1, "2 high".
- **A second route, reported by `privacy-security-reviewer` and not re-run by us:**
  `lockfileDir: sub`, pointing at an empty lockfile, also made the audit exit 0. It was not
  shown to get through `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` in CI.
- **Not tried:** `httpsProxy`, `strictSsl` and `ca` in the workspace file, as routes.

#### The fix (red `dac3614`, green `bc5a6f9`, before the squash)

- **The list.** `pnpm-workspace.yaml`'s top-level keys may be only `packages` and `catalog`
  (`WORKSPACE_SETTINGS`), and a new function, `workspaceUnlisted`, refuses every other key.
  BUG-65's `workspaceRoutes` and its assertions are unchanged.
- **How a key is named.** The way pnpm's js-yaml reads it: decoded, aliases followed. `<<` is
  refused as a key: `yaml`, the parser the test uses, keeps it as a key where js-yaml would
  merge it, so the test refuses both the `<<` and the key that holds the anchor.
  *Corrected the same day, later: "the way pnpm's js-yaml reads it" was not true for a lone
  CR* (a carriage return with no line feed after it). pnpm's reader breaks the line there
  and `yaml` keeps the CR inside a plain scalar, so a `registries:` key after a lone CR was a
  key to pnpm and nothing to this check. That is the BLOCK `privacy-security-reviewer` returned
  on #105 at `e57543d` (the entry at the end of this day). The reader agreed with pnpm's on
  every fixture tried; a lone CR was not one of them.
- **Adding a setting** is a change to this test, and `/scripts/` is code-owned, so the owner
  approves it.
- **Refused** (each is a fixture in the test): `registries` (default and scoped), `registry`,
  `lockfileDir`, `overrides`, `httpsProxy`, `strictSsl`, `ca`, a plain `auditConfig`, quoted and
  escaped `registries`, a sequence key, a `${…}` key, an alias key, a `<<` merge, an
  unparsable file, and a top-level sequence. **Passes:** today's file.
- **Tests.** The file has 44 tests: the 41 that stood at `c37d0f0`, and three named
  `BUG-67:`. The red commit fails only its own new test (the coordinating session): 1
  failed, 41 passed.
- **BUG-25.** The list also refuses an `overrides:` key in the workspace file, which is
  BUG-25's route through that file (pinned by a fixture). By the same rule it refuses
  `packageExtensions` and `patchedDependencies` there; no fixture names those. BUG-25 is not
  done: the rest of its row, the lockfile's `link:` and `file:` resolutions, is not checked by
  this. One sentence in BUG-25's row says so.
- **`code-reviewer`'s tidy-ups** (`62c2ce2`): the `scalarText` helper, identical to `String`,
  is inlined, and a no-op `i` flag is dropped from `rcNames`' regex.

#### Checks on the final test file

- `scripts/dependency-audit.test.mjs`: 44 of 44 (`test-author`; I ran it at `62c2ce2`: 44
  passed). The whole of `scripts/`: 1875 of 1875 (`test-author`).
- The dependency-audit tests and the decision tests together: 61 of 61 (the coordinating
  session).
- `pnpm run tests:changes`: "1 changed test file(s), none weakened (RG-03)" (I ran it).
- **Not run:** `gate:quick` (the coordinating session runs it before the push; no result is
  in this record), `gate:full`, CI and the five reviewers on these three commits.
  `4577ecf` passed CI and all five reviewers before BUG-67 was found (the coordinating
  session's report).

#### What changed in the records

- `docs/progress.md`: the BUG-66 row (the "did not redirect" statement corrected); a BUG-67
  row after it; the BUG-25 row (one sentence); the code-owner paragraph (a short BUG-67
  paragraph); the gotcha with the next free ID (BUG-68; BUG-13 to BUG-67 are taken); "In
  flight" (its first line, a sentence in BUG-66's paragraph, BUG-67's paragraph, and the
  next free IDs, BUG-68 and D-144).
- `docs/progress/m3.md`: this entry, and three corrections marked in place in the entry
  above (the "where it stands" paragraph, the BUG-66 section's "did not redirect" line, and
  the checks' counts).
- `docs/plan/README.md`: not changed. It names BUG-64 and no later bug (searched).
- `docs/plan/decisions.md`: not changed. D-144 is not written.

#### What is unverified here

#### BUG-68, the bug

- **CI's `security` job installs, then audits.** `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` and then
  `pnpm audit --audit-level high` (`ci.yml`, lines 786 and 788). pnpm 10.33.0 runs each workspace
  package's install scripts (`preinstall`, `install`, `postinstall`, `prepare`) during the
  install. Such a script can rewrite the root `.npmrc` (BUG-66's redirect) or write to
  `$GITHUB_ENV`, after the checked-in files were checked and before the audit runs.
- **The same script would also run before `licenses:check` and gitleaks in that job** (the
  reviewer's reading; the step order is read above, the effect on those steps is not shown).
- **Found by `privacy-security-reviewer` on #105,** at `892d7c9` and again at `82c4be0`. It
  reproduced it twice with pnpm 10.33.0. Its finding at `82c4be0` was a should-fix: the test's
  "Not pinned" list said "Queued as its own bug" and no record said so.
- **Confirmed by the coordinating session** on 2026-10-26, in a minimal pnpm 10.33.0 workspace. A
  `postinstall` in a workspace package ran during `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` and appended a
  `registry=` line to the root `.npmrc`. With `--ignore-scripts` it did not run.
- **Not run by anyone, as far as I was told:** the whole chain in one go (an install script, then
  the audit). BUG-66's reproduction already showed that a `registry=` line in `.npmrc` makes the
  audit exit 0 against a server that reports nothing, and the two halves above are each shown;
  the join is not.
- **Today's exposure.** No workspace package has an install script (I read the keys, above).
  The root's `prepare` is in the code-owned `/package.json` and only runs `git config`.
  `apps/server/package.json` and `packages/contracts/package.json` have no CODEOWNERS entry
  (read), where `/package.json` and `/apps/mobile/package.json` do.
- **#105 did not cause it.** It is on `main` already, and #105's test lists it under "Not pinned".

#### BUG-68, the planned fix (its own pull request, after #105 and D-144)

- *Widened 2026-10-26, after the second BLOCK on `1f7e664` (`privacy-security-reviewer`'s S1): `--ignore-scripts` on every
  install before a required check, not on `security`'s alone, `ai-review.yml:180` included (a hand merge, D-075); the test
  below is a second line only, since it runs after the install (the last section of the BUG-69 entry).*
- The `security` job runs `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --ignore-scripts`; check that
  `licenses:check` still works with it.
- A test refuses `preinstall`, `install`, `postinstall`, `prepare` (and their pre and post
  forms) in workspace `package.json` files.
- The "Not pinned" line in the test file names no bug ID ("Queued as its own bug"; `82c4be0`'s
  message says an ID "is given when it is recorded"). The fix's pull request is where it moves
  from "not pinned" to a test, and where the line changes (`test-author`'s file).
- **A pointer for whoever writes it.** `progress/m2.md`, under "LOST-02 on the pull request, and
  merged", records that after an install with `--ignore-scripts` the jest-expo drill of
  `gate:quick` failed locally ("Cannot find module … jest/bin/jest.js") and passed after an install
  without the flag; the `.bin` shim's path was the likely cause, "observed, not proven". The
  `security` job runs no Jest, so it may not matter here. It is a reason to run the fix's
  `licenses:check` and the other steps of the job, not only to read the flag.

#### For the owner, not decided

- **Should `apps/server/package.json` and `packages/contracts/package.json` also get a code
  owner?** The owner's D-144 answer named only `pnpm-workspace.yaml` and `pnpm-lock.yaml`; this

#### The BLOCK

- **Found by `privacy-security-reviewer`** on #105 at `e57543d`. It cites `7391b70`, GitHub's
  test-merge commit of `e57543d` into `main` (the coordinating session read its parents).
- **The hole.** pnpm 10.33.0 reads `pnpm-workspace.yaml` with its bundled `@zkochan/js-yaml`
  0.0.11, which treats a lone CR (a carriage return with no line feed after it) as a line break.
  `yaml` 2.9.1, the parser the test used, keeps it inside a plain scalar, with no error or warning.
- **The attack.** The line `  - packages/*`, then a lone CR, then
  `registries: {default: "<local server>"}` hides a top-level `registries` key from every check
  in the test, while pnpm reads it. It is BUG-67's route with a different way to write the key.
- **Reproduced by the coordinating session** on 2026-10-26, two ways:
  - with that edit in the repository's own `pnpm-workspace.yaml` (restored afterwards and checked with
    `cmp`), `scripts/dependency-audit.test.mjs` passed **44 of 44**: the test did not see it;
  - with the same edit in a scratch copy and a local server that reports zero vulnerabilities,
    `pnpm audit --audit-level high` exited 0, "No known vulnerabilities found", and the server
    logged the audit POST. The control exits 1.
- **Also reported by the reviewer, not re-run by us:** a `lockfileDir:` line behind the same CR.
- **The fourth time.** Each earlier review round found another way the test's model of YAML differed
  from pnpm's: escapes and the environment fill (BUG-65), a flow mapping (pinned in `60d09ec`), and now
  the lone CR. That is why the fix below is not one more case for the parser.

#### The reviewer's second should-fix, and the wrong bundle

- **The should-fix.** The test's comments named `@zkochan/js-yaml` 0.0.9 as pnpm's reader
  (`privacy-security-reviewer`). The entry of 2026-10-26 above, "BUG-65 and BUG-66", says it too, and
  carries a correction note now.
- **What the coordinating session found while checking it.** `/opt/node-tools/node_modules/pnpm` is
  pnpm **10.28.0**, and its bundle holds `@zkochan/js-yaml` 0.0.9. The pnpm this repository runs is
  10.33.0 (`packageManager` is `pnpm@10.33.0`; `pnpm --version` says 10.33.0), and its bundle has only
  0.0.11. As the test's header puts it, the launcher in `/opt/node-tools` hands over to the pinned version, so
  a `pnpm` command runs 10.33.0 and its bundle is not the one on that path.
- **What that makes wrong.** `test-author` read the 10.28.0 bundle while saying "10.33.0". So the
  claims in the test file, in its pushed commit messages from `96c7156` (BUG-65's green) on, and in the
  records were really checked against 10.28.0. In the records: "pnpm's own parser is 0.0.9" and the
  workflow read, in the BUG-65 section above, and the BUG-65 row in `progress.md`. Each now has a
  *Corrected the same day* note.
- **What stands.** The scratch end-to-end runs (routes (a) to (d), the registry server, the
  `registries:` key, the lone CR) used the real `pnpm` command, which is 10.33.0. Their results do not
  depend on the bundle that was read.
- **Re-checked against the 10.33.0 bundle by `test-author`** in this round (as reported to me):
  - the workspace file is read by `read-yaml-file` 2.1.0 (it strips a BOM) with `@zkochan/js-yaml`
    0.0.11;
  - settings go through `replaceEnvInSettings` (`@pnpm/config.env-replace` 3.0.2);
  - the root `.npmrc` goes through `@pnpm/npm-conf` 3.0.2, config-chain and `ini` 1.3.8, byte-identical
    to 10.28.0's copy;
  - every YAML fixture in the test was re-read with 0.0.11, and there are no disagreements beyond those
    the test's header documents: a `<<` merge, an unknown tag, and now the lone CR and an undefined
    alias `*x` (pnpm's reader throws on it, and `yaml`'s `parseDocument` does not report it);
  - BUG-11's first two items hold in 10.33.0. **Its pnpmfile item was not re-read**, and the test's
    comment says so.
- **D-143.** Its sentence "read in pnpm 10.33.0's bundle" (the ignore is global) was re-checked and
  holds, so `decisions.md` is not touched.
- **Not recorded: which bundle `test-auditor` read** for "the workspace file's keys are copied onto the
  config; the audit uses `opts.registries.default`" (BUG-67's entry above). The route itself stands on
  the end-to-end runs of the coordinating session and the reviewer, which used `pnpm` 10.33.0.
- **The workflow read** in the BUG-65 section ("read in pnpm's bundle and not run") was not reported
  as re-read in 10.33.0's bundle, so it stays unverified there.

#### The fix (test-only, `scripts/dependency-audit.test.mjs`; commits before the squash)

- **`2ea9e6c` (red).** Three new tests fail on the old code: 3 failed, 44 passed (the coordinating
  session ran it).
- **`41d9bfc` (green).** Three layers are added and nothing is removed:
  - **(a) A lone CR is refused.** `decodedYaml` refuses any CR with no LF after it, in the workspace
    file and in the workflows (a workflow's lone CR is refused because the two readings differ; it
    is not shown to change a GitHub run).
  - **(b) A line grammar, with no YAML parser.** `workspaceLines` checks `pnpm-workspace.yaml`'s raw
    text, split on LF only, against a strict grammar, `WORKSPACE_LINE`. Allowed: blank lines;
    whole-line comments; `packages:` and `catalog:`, each once; plain globs under `packages:`; plain
    `name: range` entries under `catalog:`. Any CR (CRLF included), NEL, LS or PS fails.
    - **This is the class fix.** Every round had found another way the test's YAML model differed from
      pnpm's, and a grammar on the raw text needs no model.
    - **The grammar test shows it is load-bearing.** It has 20 refused fixtures and records which of
      them the YAML-reading checks (`workspaceRoutes` and `workspaceUnlisted`) let through: a CRLF copy,
      a flow mapping, a double-quoted key, an anchor and alias, a tag, an inline comment, a NEL, an LS,
      a PS, and `*/apps`. (I counted the fixtures and read the list in the test.) Some of those set
      nothing pnpm reads; each is a shape the test need not read.
  - **(c) The root `.npmrc` is pinned** to exactly `engine-strict=true` and a newline, compared byte
    for byte (`code-reviewer`'s suggestion on `e57543d`).
- **`1ecf157`.**
  - `readRc()` throws on a missing root `.npmrc`, at all five places that read it. `test-auditor`'s
    should-fix on `e57543d`: renaming the path had passed all 44 tests, and now 5 fail (the
    coordinating session's report of the rename; I did not run it). It is also `code-reviewer`'s
    tidy-up of the repeated read.
  - The test's header names BUG-68 for the install-script route.
- **Tests.** 49: the 44 that stood, and five new, all named `BUG-67:` or `BUG-66, BUG-67:`: the lone
  CR in the workspace file, the lone CR in a workflow, the line grammar's refusals, today's file against
  the grammar, and the `.npmrc` byte pin.

#### Checks

- **With the reviewer's exact edit applied to the repository's real `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, 5 tests now
  fail** (the coordinating session; file restored). Before the fix it passed 44 of 44.
- `scripts/dependency-audit.test.mjs`: **49 of 49** (`test-author`; I ran it at `1ecf157`: 49 passed).

#### The BLOCK

- **The verdict.** BLOCK, in the comment headed "PR #105 (head 357cd4f)", the test-merge of `ec81f65` into `main`.
- **The finding, in the reviewer's words and mine.** Every new check is a vitest test, reached in CI through
  `pnpm run test:unit`. pnpm 10.33.0 reads `scriptShell` from `pnpm-workspace.yaml` and `script-shell` from
  `.npmrc`, and neither file had a code owner. One line in either makes `pnpm run` hand every script to a shell the
  author chooses. The reviewer's scenario: a pull request that touches only `pnpm-workspace.yaml` and adds
  `auditConfig: {ignoreGhsas: […]}` and `scriptShell: /bin/true`. Then `security` is green with the ignores, and
  `unit`, which holds the guard, is green having run nothing, and so are `gate-integrity`, `static`, `system`,
  `contract`, `traceability` and `mutation`.
- **Why it blocks now,** in the reviewer's words: `main` is open to this route already, but the owner put D-144

#### The reproduction

- **By the coordinating session** (told to me, not repeated), pnpm 10.33.0, in a minimal project with a script that
  echoes and exits 1:
  - control: exit 1, and the script ran;
  - `scriptShell: /bin/true` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml`: exit 0, and the script did not run;
  - `script-shell=/bin/true` in `.npmrc`: exit 0, not run;
  - **a root `.pnpmfile.cjs` with an `updateConfig` hook returning `{ ...config, scriptShell: '/bin/true' }`: exit 0,
    not run.** This is a third route, beyond the reviewer's report. 10.33.0's default pnpmfile name is only
    `.pnpmfile.cjs`; the coordinating session checked that in the bundle;
  - a package's own `.npmrc` with `script-shell` hijacked a run only from inside that package's folder. From the
    root (`--filter`, `-r`) the script ran and failed. No package has one (my `git ls-files`, above). *Corrected
    later the same day: turbo runs each package's script from that package's folder, so such a file does reach the
    typecheck; open on `main` too (the last section of this entry).*
- **By the reviewer** (reported, not repeated): in a `git archive` copy of `357cd4f` with `scriptShell: /bin/true`,
  `pnpm run test:unit` and `pnpm run licenses:check` exited 0 having run nothing, while the test file run directly with
  vitest failed as it should.

#### The package-folder route (`privacy-security-reviewer`'s S1), now verified

- **The claim that was open.** The entry above, and D-144, listed "whether turbo runs a package's script from that package's
  folder" as not verified, because the one reproduction had a package's own `.npmrc` hijack a run only from inside that
  folder, and the root's `--filter` and `-r` runs ran the script and failed.
- **What the reviewer found.** Turbo runs each package's script from the package's folder. With a deliberate type error in
  `apps/server/src` and `apps/server/.npmrc` holding `script-shell=/bin/true`, `pnpm run typecheck` gave "5 successful, 5
  total" and exit 0, and the error got through; without the line it failed with TS2322. A package-level `scriptShell` in
  `pnpm-workspace.yaml` did the same; a package-level `.pnpmfile.cjs` alone did not. Its list of what is not affected:
  commands started from the root with `--filter … run` (the mobile jest suite in `test:unit`), the audit, the licence check,
  the unit, system and integration tests, and the AI reviews.
- **Reproduced by the coordinating session** on this repository with pnpm 10.33.0 (told to me, not repeated): with a
  temporary type error in `apps/server/src`, `pnpm exec turbo run typecheck --filter=@trygghverdag/server --force` exits 2
  (TS2322); with `apps/server/.npmrc` holding `script-shell=/bin/true` added, it exits 0, "4 successful", with no TS2322. Both
  probe files were removed. (The reviewer's "5 successful" and the coordinating session's "4 successful" are two different
  commands; each is as its author reported it.)
- **So** a package's own settings file can make that package's typecheck pass having run nothing. **It was open on `main`
  too,** and "who must approve these files today: nobody" (the reviewer: `apps/*/.npmrc`, `packages/contracts/.npmrc` and
  `apps/server/pnpm-workspace.yaml` have no code owner, and nothing rejected an `.npmrc` outside the root).

  `.pnpmfile.cjs` in the repository but the root's two and the spike's workspace file, by a walk of the tree with no git
  subprocess (`node_modules` and `.git` skipped). It runs in the root Vitest run, which starts from the root; the test's comment
  says the files it refuses cannot switch it off for that reason, and the root's own files and config are code-owned. The
  coordinating session probed it: with `apps/server/.npmrc` added it fails, 1 failed and 52 passed, and the file was removed.
  `test-author` reports that in a scratch copy `apps/server/.npmrc`, `packages/contracts/pnpm-workspace.yaml` and

  `RC_SETTINGS` (the byte pin compares one exact string and needs none). The header says these checks protect only while

#### The BLOCK, B1

- **The route.** `pnpm-workspace.yaml` and the root `package.json`'s `workspaces` took in `apps/*` and `packages/*`. A pull
  request that added only `apps/<new>/package.json` created a workspace package, and no CODEOWNERS line matches that path
  (the last match wins), so no owner approved it. Its `preinstall` or `postinstall` runs during
  `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` in every required job, and in `ai-review.yml:180`'s install. It can write
  `script-shell=/bin/true` to the root `.npmrc`, so every later `pnpm run` passes having run nothing. That is BUG-69's route by a
  way D-144 did not cover.
- **The lockfile does not stop it.** With the repository's real files and an added `apps/evil/package.json` with no
  dependencies, the reviewer got "Lockfile is up to date, resolution step is skipped". Its probe on the real repository stopped
  offline while fetching packages, after printing that line; its minimal-workspace probes ran to the end.
- **Reproduced by the coordinating session** (told to me, not repeated), in a minimal pnpm 10.33.0 workspace:
  - with `apps/*`, a package added after the lockfile, with a `preinstall` that appends `script-shell=/bin/true` to the root
    `.npmrc`: `CI=1 pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` exits 0 and runs it, and the next `pnpm run` of a failing script exits 0
    without running;
  - with the packages named, the same install leaves `.npmrc` alone ("Scope: all 2 workspace projects"), and the failing script
    exits 1.
- **Neither planned follow-up closed it.** BUG-68's plan was `--ignore-scripts` in `security` only, and BUG-69's check before
  pnpm runs before the install, so before the script writes `.npmrc`.
- **Not #105's doing:** the route is open on `main`. The records were wrong to say it was covered.

#### The records that were wrong

- **D-144:** "With these two, every workspace package's manifest has an owner" held only for the five packages that existed.
  The paragraph is corrected in place with a dated note (D-144 is unmerged, and this is a statement of fact, not the decision).
- **The BUG-68 row:** "D-144 gives both an owner" had the same flaw, and the row's plan was `security`-only. Both corrected.
- **The "D-144, written in this commit" bullet of the entry above** ("With the two manifests, every workspace package's manifest
  has an owner"): a *Corrected* note.
- **What the owner was told.** The owner's answer 3 ("Ja, begge (Recommended)", the two manifests) was given when the records
  said owning them covered BUG-68's route, and the BUG-68 row said so. It covered the two packages that existed. The fix below
  changes none of what the owner decided and adds no path to D-144; the owner reads it in `progress.md`'s owner section.

#### The fix (Claude's choice under D-144, D-031; only owned files, no path added)

- **`a3170ff` (`test-author`, red).** Three tests in `scripts/dependency-audit.test.mjs`, beside BUG-69's walk, with
  `WORKSPACE_FOLDERS` (`apps/mobile`, `apps/server`, `packages/config`, `packages/contracts`, `packages/test-kit`): both lists
  (`pnpm-workspace.yaml`'s `packages:`, read as the YAML parser reads it, and `package.json`'s `workspaces`) name exactly the
  five, with no glob; each named folder has a `package.json` that is an owner path (a folder entry such as `/packages/config/`
  counts); every folder under `apps/` and `packages/` with a `package.json` is named in both lists. Red: both lists were
  `["apps/*", "packages/*"]`. `test-author` reports that with the fix applied in a scratch copy eight test files pass, 687 of
  687, and `imports:check` passes after an offline install and fails when the two lists differ.
- **`586416f` (`implementer`).** `pnpm-workspace.yaml`'s `packages:` and `package.json`'s `workspaces` name the five, sorted, with
  a comment saying why. `catalog:` is unchanged. The implementer reports `CI=1 pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --offline` says
  "Scope: all 6 workspace projects" and "Lockfile is up to date" (five packages and, I take it, the root). I read that the lockfile is unchanged.
- **What it means.** A new package needs a change to two owned files, so D-144's claim, "every workspace package's manifest has an
  owner", holds from #105's merge. I did not look for another way to get a package outside the five installed; the one I know
  of is BUG-25's, `link:` and `file:` resolutions in the lockfile, which is owned.
- **`014855b` (`test-author`, on `test-auditor`'s PASS on `1f7e664`).** `test-auditor`'s S1: pnpm 11 looks for `.pnpmfile.mjs`
  first (its reading of pnpm 11.25.0's bundle, not run), and nothing pinned pnpm's major, so a move to pnpm 11 would leave a root
  `.pnpmfile.mjs` with no owner. A test beside `D144_FILES` pins `package.json`'s `packageManager` to `/^pnpm@10\./`, and its
  message says a move to another major must first give each default pnpmfile name an owner (`/.pnpmfile.mjs` for pnpm 11).
  `/package.json` is owned, so the move itself needs the owner. `test-author` reports it fails on `pnpm@11.0.0` in a scratch copy.
  N3: the walk skips `.stryker-tmp`. And `scripts/drills.test.mjs` read every `workspaces` entry as `folder/*`, so it found no
  package once the entries named folders; it now reads a plain folder as itself and `folder/*` as before, with no assertion
  changed (RG-03). `test-auditor`'s suggestion that the check before pnpm refuse any root file matching `/pnpmfile/i` is in the
  BUG-69 row. Its N4, that no test shows the walk does not follow a symbolic link to a folder, is low impact and open.

#### BUG-68, wider than planned (`privacy-security-reviewer`'s S1 on `1f7e664`)

- `--ignore-scripts` belongs on every `pnpm install` that runs before a required check, not on `security`'s alone. `ci.yml` has
  ten (lines above), and `ai-review.yml:180` runs the pull request's own code in a job with `id-token: write` and
  `pull-requests: write`, before the step that is given the Claude secrets. **Changing `ai-review.yml` is a hand merge
  (D-075),** so BUG-68's pull request needs the owner for it.
- The planned test that refuses install scripts in workspace `package.json` files runs after the install, so it is a second
  line only. The BUG-68 row and D-144's "Not in this decision" say so now.

#### The three commits

- **`576efa4` (`test-author`, red).** The new `scripts/install-scripts.test.mjs`, six tests: three checks, each with a fixture
  test. (1) Every `pnpm install` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (`install`, `i`, `add`, `install-test`, `it`) must carry
  `--ignore-scripts` on that same command, with no `false` after it and no other spelling beside it; `ci.yml` is decoded as
  GitHub reads it, every string, and each string is split as the shell splits it; red for all ten. (2) No workspace
  `package.json` (the five folders `pnpm-workspace.yaml` names) holds a script pnpm 10.33.0 runs on install, or a `binding.gyp`:
  `preinstall`, `install`, `postinstall`, `preprepare`, `prepare`, `postprepare`, `prepublish` and `prepublishOnly`, which
  `test-author` read in pnpm 10.33.0's bundle and ran in a minimal workspace (reported, not repeated by me). (3) The root
  `package.json`'s only install-time script is `prepare`, exactly `git config core.hooksPath .githooks` with its `or true`
  fallback, with no `pnpm:devPreinstall` and no root `binding.gyp`. (2) and (3) passed already. The commit also changed one "Not
  pinned" comment bullet in `scripts/dependency-audit.test.mjs` (RG-03, comment only, no assertion).
- **`753553e` (`test-author`).** The CI-03 drill in `scripts/drills.test.mjs` now puts the package's own `node_modules/.bin` on
  `PATH`, as pnpm does, instead of a scratch symlink of it. The RG-03 reason is in the message and no assertion changed (the
  failing sample must still turn the runner red in its own summary, with the passing neighbour as the control, and the
  stand-ins must still not pass for it). **Why the flag made it necessary:** after an install with `--ignore-scripts`, pnpm does
  not rewrite the 34 `.bin` shims through the projects' symlinks (`linkBins`, in `runLifecycleHooksConcurrently`). A shim then
  reads `$basedir/../../../../node_modules/.pnpm/jest@…`, which from the drill's scratch folder resolved to `/node_modules/…`:
  "Cannot find module". The real `test:unit` was never affected: it reaches jest through `pnpm --filter`, and jest's 706 tests
  passed with the flag. A probe, `jest --ci || true` in place of the app's test script, still turns the drill red.
- **`258941a` (`implementer`).** All ten `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` lines in `ci.yml` become `pnpm install
  --frozen-lockfile --ignore-scripts`, and a header comment gives the reason. Nothing else changed. **What the flag skips:** the
  root's `prepare`, which only sets local git hooks (no CI job uses them); dependency build scripts were already skipped by
  pnpm 10's default ("Ignored build scripts: cpu-features, esbuild ×3, protobufjs, ssh2", the coordinating session's report).

`progress/m2.md`, in the LOST-02 entry ("LOST-02 on the pull request, and merged (2026-10-04 and 2026-10-05, #63, `5cd5d24`)"),
under "Found on the way", recorded that after `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --ignore-scripts`, `gate:quick` failed the INF-10
jest-expo drill with "Cannot find module '/node_modules/.pnpm/jest@29.7.0_@types+node@26.6.2/node_modules/jest/bin/jest.js'", that
the drill passed after an install without the flag "as CI installs", and that the `.bin` shim's path was the likely cause, "observed,
not proven". **It is now explained by `753553e`'s cause above, and reproduced** (`test-author`'s report, not repeated by me): swapping
in only the flagged `jest` shim made the drill fail, and restoring it made it pass. `m2.md`'s "as CI installs" described CI as it was;
from this pull request CI installs with the flag too, and the drill holds either way (15 CI-03 drill tests passed after an install
with and without the flag, per `test-author`).

- **Not run by anyone:** `test:integration` (no Docker), `gate:integrity` (it needs tokens), the mutation legs and `android-e2e`.

### 2026-10-27 — [#106](https://github.com/bvst/TryggHverdag/pull/106) (BUG-68): the review round on `db75070`, and what "fixed" means

**Where it stands, written so that it is true now.** #106 is open, on `claude/busy-faraday-40n2zl`, with head `db75070`
(GitHub's REST API, read 2026-10-27). It holds BUG-68's `ci.yml` part only: the ten installs in `ci.yml` take
`--ignore-scripts`. **The `ai-review.yml:180` part is open,** to batch with Dependabot #102 as a hand merge under D-075, and
`daily-status.yml:81` is out of scope. The two entries above of today say BUG-68 is "fixed"; they meant the `ci.yml` part, and
this is the wording to read them by (`privacy-security-reviewer`'s S2, below). They are left as they were written, as history.
Three reviewers have passed `db75070`; the should-fixes below are being made in the next push and **are not made yet.**

**What I checked, and what I was told.** On 2026-10-27 I read #106 and its three review comments through GitHub's REST API
(posted 07:08:07, 07:09:50 and 07:09:55 UTC, by the AI reviewers' bot). After the edits I ran only Prettier on the three docs files and five record-reading test files (206 of 206); I opened no job log. The
list of what is made, kept and noted is the coordinating session's; the findings it refers to are as the comments word them.

#### The reviews of `db75070`: all three PASS

- **`code-reviewer`: PASS,** no blocking finding. It ran `install-scripts.test.mjs` and `dependency-audit.test.mjs` (62 of 62); it
  did not run `drills.test.mjs`. It counted ten `pnpm install` commands in `ci.yml`, all with the flag, found no
  `onlyBuiltDependencies` entry or `.npmrc` setting that CI needs a build script for, and found the drill change acceptable
  (RG-03: a test edit with a written reason, assertions unchanged, in its own `test(...)` commit). Three should-fixes:
  `scripts/install-scripts.test.mjs` is about 650 lines (a hand-written shell tokenizer, a YAML walker, a fixture) for one flag
  on ten lines, and a much smaller test would protect as well; the lifecycle names are written out a second time as a mirror,
  which adds little; and the `INSTALL_LIFECYCLE` comment cites a path on one machine that nobody else can open (name only the
  pnpm version and the bundle function names).
- **`test-auditor`: PASS** (audited at `db75070`). `gate:integrity` 5 of 5, 13 required checks; `req:coverage` 28 of 63 with
  `docs/requirements-status.md` unchanged; `tests:changes` 3 changed test files, none weakened; `mutation` green;
  `coverage-baseline.json` not in the diff; the six new tests pass at HEAD. It planted 28 faults in memory and 20 were caught.
  `unit`, `traceability`, `static`, `integration`, `android-e2e` and three `ai-review` checks were still running when it read, and
  it did not wait or re-run them. Two should-fixes, both in the new test's own fixtures: **(1)** row 30
  (`run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile # --ignore-scripts`) has its comment removed by YAML before the shell splitter sees it, so
  removing the comment stripping at line 259 still passes all six tests; the fix is the same line inside a `run: |` block,
  expected to be refused. **(2)** the plain-text second pass (`plainInstallLines`) is only ever asserted to return `[]`, so making
  it return nothing passes; the fix is to assert the exact line numbers it finds in the fixture, that it does not return the
  comment line, and that its count on `ci.yml` equals the installs found.
- **`privacy-security-reviewer`: PASS.** It found no PRIV or SEC violation, no weakened check, and nothing CI needs that stops
  running; it read the check runs of `db75070` and did not open a job log. **S1:** `ai-review.yml:180` installs with install
  scripts on, in a `pull_request` job with `id-token: write` and `pull-requests: write`, before the step that gets the Claude
  secrets; not new, tracked for the #102 batch; the fix there is the flag plus `installsIn` extended to cover `ai-review.yml`.
  **S2:** the records called BUG-68 "fixed" while the `ai-review.yml` leg is open; its suggested wording was "ci.yml leg fixed;
  ai-review.yml leg open (with #102)", and the records now read that way (this commit). **N1:** `npx pnpm@10.33.0 install` gets past
  the check, because the word `pnpm@…` does not match; a scratch-copy probe caught 4 of 6 bad changes, and `pnpm rebuild` is already
  listed as not pinned; the fix is to match `/^pnpm(@\S+)?$/` or to list `npx` and `dlx` under "Not pinned". N2: nothing CI
  depends on goes with the flag (dependency build scripts were already off; what goes is the root `prepare` and pnpm's re-linking
  of the `.bin` shims). N3: the drill change does not weaken it. **N4:** `daily-status.yml:81` also installs without the flag;
  low risk, since it runs on a schedule or by manual dispatch; add the flag the next time that file is touched. Not verified by
  it: whether CI is green with the flag, since five checks were still running; `drills.test.mjs` was not run and nothing was
  installed with or without the flag.

#### What is being done, and what is not (the coordinating session's list)

- **Should-fixes being made in the next push:** `test-auditor`'s two fixture-strength points; `privacy-security-reviewer`'s N1
  (`npx pnpm@10.33.0 install`); `code-reviewer`'s machine-specific path in the `INSTALL_LIFECYCLE` comment.
- **Kept as they are, by Claude's call:** `code-reviewer`'s suggestions to shrink the shell tokenizer and to delete the mirrored
  lifecycle list. `test-auditor`'s points pull the other way: they ask for more fixtures and more assertions on the same checker,
  and the mirror is what fails when a name is dropped from `INSTALL_LIFECYCLE`. This is a judgement, not a check, and the owner
  has not been asked.
- **Noted, for later:** `privacy-security-reviewer`'s N4 (the flag on `daily-status.yml:81` the next time that file is touched)
  and S1 (the `ai-review.yml` batch with #102 must get opened; `code-reviewer` says the same).

#### What changed in the records

- `docs/progress.md` (the header, the BUG-68 row, the owner section, "In flight") and `docs/plan/README.md` (the header, the bug
  paragraph, the M3 summary): BUG-68 reads "the `ci.yml` part is fixed in #106; the `ai-review.yml:180` part is open, to batch
  with Dependabot #102 (a hand merge, D-075)"; #106 is named; the S1 and N4 items are in.
- `docs/progress/m3.md`: this entry. `docs/plan/decisions.md` is not changed.

#### What is unverified here

- **That the should-fixes are made, and that they pass review:** none is pushed yet.
- **That CI is green on `db75070`:** the reviewers read it while checks were running; I did not read the final state.
- **The reviewers' own runs** (the 28 planted faults, the 4 of 6 probe, the 62 of 62): their reports, not repeated by me.
- **The judgement to keep the tokenizer and the mirror:** Claude's call, as the coordinating session gave it.

`ai-review.yml:180` part is open, to batch with Dependabot #102 (a hand merge, D-075), so BUG-68 itself stays open.

the four comments on #106 posted since 07:21 UTC (the reply and the three reviews, 07:22:41, 07:23:56 and 07:25:25 UTC); the
header of `scripts/install-scripts.test.mjs` (its "Not pinned" list, lines 60 to 75); and `.github/workflows/daily-status.yml`,
whose triggers are `schedule` (line 34) and `workflow_dispatch` (line 39). I opened no job log, and ran nothing for this entry
but Prettier and five record-reading test files after the edits. **I did not try a dispatch.** The list of what is being fixed
and answered is the coordinating session's.

#### Round 2: the reviews of `4830acc` (all three PASS)

- **`code-reviewer`: PASS.** (1) The simplicity point again: the test is about 700 lines, and about 60 would do (parse `ci.yml`
  with `yaml`, require the flag on every line that installs, check the manifests for lifecycle scripts). (2) For the two installs
  left out (`ai-review.yml:180` and `daily-status.yml:81`), make sure a follow-up BUG ID exists and is linked from `m3.md`. (3) The
  14-line RG-03 comment in `scripts/drills.test.mjs` is much longer than the one-line change it explains; cut it to about 4, and
  confirm that `test-author` made that edit. Notes: no `onlyBuiltDependencies` anywhere, so no dependency build script ran in CI
  before, and skipping the root `prepare` is harmless; it only skimmed `docs/progress.md` and did not review the README or
  `docs/progress/m3.md`.
- **`privacy-security-reviewer`: PASS,** "nothing has to be fixed before merge". Round 1's N1 (`npx pnpm@10.33.0 install`) is
  fixed: `PNPM_WORD` at `install-scripts.test.mjs:313` and fixture rows 36 to 42. **N1** (now): `ai-review.yml:180` still installs
  with scripts on; leaving it for the #102 batch is acceptable (each pull request runs its own copy of the workflow, fork pull
  requests get no secrets, and anyone who can add a postinstall can already edit the workflow), and the batch should add the flag and
  extend the test's file list to `ai-review.yml`. **N2:** the test does not see `pnpm/action-setup`'s `run_install` input; the
  action at pin `ea17c68` can run `pnpm install` itself with scripts on, no workflow sets it today, and an assertion that none
  does (or only `false`) is suggested. **N3:** the records overstate how safe `daily-status.yml:81` is (below). **N4:** the drill's
  `PATH` change has no security effect.
- **`test-auditor`: PASS.** One should-fix, RG-01: the header says the plain-text pass means "a part of the file the decoded
  reading misses cannot hide one", but the two readings are not independent. Both drop comments with the same regex
  (`/(^|\s)#.*$/`, lines 262 and 352), and the splitter does not split on `<` or `>`. Two silent misses, run by it against the
  file's own helpers: `run: echo "build #1" && pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` (neither reading finds an install, so the counts
  agree) and `run: pnpm install>log` (a redirect with no space). The fix it gives: split on `<` and `>`, strip comments only
  outside quotes in at least one reading, add both lines to the fixture as refused, or else list them under "Not pinned" and soften
  the header. Notes: the drill change does not weaken it (RG-03); `gate:integrity` 5 of 5; `req:coverage` 28 of 63 with
  `docs/requirements-status.md` unchanged; `tests:changes` "3 changed test file(s), none weakened"; 13 faults planted in memory
  and 12 caught (the survivor, `own.slice(1)` to `own`, changes nothing); both of its round-1 should-fixes are closed.

#### A correction: `daily-status.yml` does not run only `main`'s reviewed code on a schedule

`privacy-security-reviewer`'s N3 on `4830acc`: the records said `daily-status.yml:81` is out of scope because it "runs `main`'s
reviewed code on a schedule" (the coordinating session's reason, which I recorded). The workflow also has `workflow_dispatch`
(line 39, read), and a manual run, by someone with write access, can be pointed at any branch and then runs with the Claude
secrets. The test header (`scripts/install-scripts.test.mjs`, lines 67 to 69) already says so: "on a schedule, from `main`'s
already-reviewed code, or when started by hand (`workflow_dispatch`)". **So the install at `daily-status.yml:81` is out of #106 and
gets the flag the next time that file is touched** (N4, round 1), and it is not "only `main`'s reviewed code". I did not try a
dispatch against another branch. **The workflow's own header comment** (lines 29 to 31) says that "scheduled and manual workflows"
run only from the default branch, which N3 contradicts; the file is under `.github/` and not mine to change here, and I did not
test which is right.
